- **Pattern worth repeating**: whenever a new workflow is created that reaches any code path using Playwright (search for `async_playwright`/`pw.chromium.launch` in the target script, not just an obvious "browser" name), copy the `playwright install --with-deps chromium` step from an existing working workflow (e.g. `match_post.yml`) — pip installing the `playwright` package alone does not install the actual browser binary the runner needs; that's a separate `playwright install` step every single Playwright-dependent workflow must have independently, and it's easy to add a new automation script that reuses an existing Playwright-based posting function without noticing the workflow YAML needs its own copy of this step.

### Code Patterns (2026-07-28, later same day) — every breaking-news blog category now announces to Facebook, not just transfers
- Follow-up to the Playwright fix above. User asked to "make sure the blogs post to Facebook as previously instructed." Audited every blog-generating path: `agent_transfer_post.py` (the "transfers" category) already builds a full fact-extracted bulletin and posts it to Telegram/Facebook/Instagram/X. `breaking_news.yml` (football, sportnews, and a rotating category — basketball/betting/tennis/cricket/rugby/boxing/f1/igaming — 3x/day) called `agent_sports_blog.py` directly and had **zero social posting of any kind** — it only wrote to `blog/posts.json` and regenerated static pages. That's the actual gap "the blogs aren't posting to Facebook" was describing: every category except transfers.
- **Fix**: added `build_facebook_caption()` / `announce_to_facebook()` to `agent_sports_blog.py` — a lightweight version of the transfer bot's pattern (no fact-extraction pass needed, just title + excerpt + link + the same per-post branded image already produced by `ensure_feature_image()` earlier in `generate_post()`), reusing `post_facebook()` from `agent3_social.py` exactly as every other posting call site in this codebase does. Wired into `run()`'s main loop right after a new post is accepted, guarded by `try/except` so a Facebook failure can never block the blog write itself (same resilience contract as `agent_transfer_post.py`'s own posting calls). Explicitly skips the `"transfers"` category if `run()` ever gets called with it (e.g. via `run_daily()`'s random category pick) — that category already gets its own richer bulletin from `agent_transfer_post.py`, and posting both would double-post the same story.
- **`breaking_news.yml` had no Facebook secrets wired in at all** (only `GROQ_API_KEY`/`GEMINI_API_KEY`/`FIRECRAWL_API_KEY`/`APIFY_TOKEN`) — added `FACEBOOK_PAGE_ID`/`FACEBOOK_PAGE_ACCESS_TOKEN` to both `env:` blocks that actually call `agent_sports_blog.py` with a real category (not the ticker-only step, which never generates a post). Without this, `post_facebook()` would have silently no-op'd with "Facebook not configured yet" even though the code path was correct.
- **Deliberately did not touch cadence**: this makes ~3 additional Facebook posts/day (whatever `breaking_news.yml` actually manages to generate — football + sportnews + 1 rotating, some runs skip a category on duplicate-title or AI-exhaustion), on top of the existing 3-post bonus/tips strategy and however many transfer-news bulletins fire that day. That's worth knowing about given the explicit 2026-07-24 "cut 13x/day to 3x/day, quality over quantity" decision earlier in this file — this change was a direct, explicit user request ("make sure you post the blogs on facebook") given in a later session, not a silent reversal of that earlier strategy. If total daily FB volume becomes a concern again, the lever to pull is `breaking_news.yml`'s cron (currently 3x/day) or removing the `announce_to_facebook()` call for specific categories, not re-adding a blanket suppression.
- **Verified**: `agent_sports_blog.py` imports cleanly and `build_facebook_caption()` renders correctly under the project's `.venv` (3.13) — did not fire a real test post to the live Page to avoid spamming it, since the underlying `post_facebook()` call and image-path handling is byte-for-byte the same function already confirmed working live via `agent_transfer_post.py` in the fix immediately before this one.

### Code Patterns (2026-07-28, later same day) — transfer_news.yml now self-pauses outside the transfer window and self-resumes when one opens
- User asked for the transfer-news bot to automatically pause once the (northern hemisphere) summer transfer window closes at the end of August, and automatically resume when a window reopens — rather than continuing to spin its ~5h50m self-perpetuating loop (see the 2026-07-27 "self-perpetuating internal loop" entry above) every hour checking for stories that mostly won't exist outside the window.
- **Fix**: added a "Check if a major transfer window is currently open" step as the very first step in the job (before checkout, so a paused run costs only a few seconds — no repo checkout, no Python setup, no dependency install). It sets a step output `open` = `true`/`false` based on UTC month: open for all of January (winter window) and June-August (summer window, closes end of August per the user's own stated cutoff), closed otherwise. Every subsequent step (`checkout`, `setup-python`, `pip install`, font install, `playwright install`, the dry-run step, and the main 5h50m loop step) got `if: steps.window.outputs.open == 'true'` added, combined with `&&` where a step already had its own `if:` condition (the dry-run/loop steps' existing `dry_run` input check). A dedicated "Paused" step with the inverse condition prints a clear message so a paused run isn't just a wall of skipped steps with no explanation in the Actions UI.
- **Manual `workflow_dispatch` always bypasses the gate** (checked via `github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch'`, short-circuits `open=true` before the date check even runs) — so a human can still manually test or force a run in the off-season without needing to fake the system clock.
- **Deliberately whole-month granularity, not exact deadline-day dates**: real deadline days shift by a day or two most years and this doesn't need to be that precise — the existing duplicate-title de-dupe in `agent_transfer_post.py` already prevents padding output even inside an open window when there's genuinely nothing new. The outer `schedule: cron: "0 * * * *"` (hourly) stays unchanged — during the off-season each hourly trigger now just runs the cheap window-check step and exits, rather than the full multi-hour loop, so "paused" is a real low-cost state, not a disabled workflow.
- **YAML gotcha hit while writing this**: `run: echo "Skipping this run entirely: no major transfer window is open. ..."` failed to parse with `mapping values are not allowed here` — a `run:` value that merely *starts with* a `"` character is not automatically a YAML-quoted scalar; it's still a **plain scalar** unless the entire value is wrapped in matching quotes with nothing before the opening quote. A colon-space (`": "`) anywhere inside a plain scalar is parsed as a nested mapping-value indicator and breaks the document. Fixed by rewording the message to avoid `": "` (used an em dash instead) rather than fighting YAML's quoting rules. **Lesson: never assume a `run: echo "..."` line is safely quoted just because double quotes are visually present — verify with `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('file.yml'))"` after any edit that adds punctuation to an inline `run:` string, especially colons.**
- **No change needed to `workflow_watchdog.yml`/`retry_failed.yml`**: a paused run exits 0 (success), not a failure, so the existing retry/watchdog safety net correctly leaves it alone during the off-season instead of mistakenly trying to "fix" the pause.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-28, later same day) — no Facebook/Telegram posts for hours because a scheduled trigger was silently dropped, and neither safety net could see it
- User reported nothing posted to Facebook/Telegram for 2-3 hours. Checked every posting workflow's run history (`gh run list --workflow=X --limit N`) rather than assuming: `accumulator_post.yml` (Telegram + Facebook, scheduled 11:00 and 18:00 UTC) had **no run at all** for its 11:00 UTC slot on 2026-07-28 — not a failure, not even an attempt, just absent from the run list entirely. `breaking_news.yml` had a real streak of failures overnight too (Groq quota exhaustion, `AIProvidersExhausted` — a pre-existing, separately-documented issue, not new) but had already recovered on its own by the time of the report; the acute cause of the report was specifically the missing accumulator run.
- **Root cause, and why both existing safety nets missed it**: this is the exact same account-wide GitHub Actions scheduler unreliability already documented for `transfer_news.yml`/`live_odds.yml`/`hourly_scrape.yml` on 2026-07-27 — GitHub silently drops the large majority of scheduled cron triggers on this account. But `workflow_watchdog.yml` only fires on a `workflow_run: completed` event, and `retry_failed.yml` only checks whether the *last* run's `conclusion` was `"failure"`. **Neither can detect "no run happened at all"** — there's no completed-with-failure event to react to when nothing ran, and the last run's conclusion just shows yesterday's success, looking completely fine. Compounding this: `accumulator_post.yml` wasn't even in either safety net's watched list to begin with (a gap already flagged as "pre-existing" in the 2026-07-24 entry above, still unfixed until now).
- **Immediate fix**: manually re-triggered `accumulator_post.yml` via `gh workflow run` to restore posting right away.
- **Durable fix — a genuinely different mechanism from both existing safety nets, not just adding accumulator_post.yml to their lists (though that was also done)**: added a "Check for missed schedules" step to `retry_failed.yml` that, for each hour-based-cron posting workflow (`accumulator_post.yml`: 11,18; `breaking_news.yml`: 7,13,19; `telegram_offers.yml`: 7; `twitter_posts.yml`: 9,13,17,21 — all UTC), computes the most recent scheduled slot that's already at least `GRACE_MIN=20` minutes old (today's slots first, falling back to yesterday's last slot if none of today's are due yet), and force-triggers the workflow via `gh workflow run` if the last actual run's `createdAt` predates that slot. This is a fundamentally different check than "did the last run fail" — it's "should a run have happened by now, and did it." High-frequency `*/N`-minute workflows (`live_odds.yml`, `hourly_scrape.yml`) and `transfer_news.yml` (already self-healing via its own internal loop, see 2026-07-27 entry) deliberately excluded — this slot model doesn't fit them.
- **Tightened `retry_failed.yml`'s own cadence from every 30 to every 15 minutes** specifically because Facebook/Telegram silence is directly user-visible — worst-case detection+recovery time is now roughly `GRACE_MIN (20min) + one 15-min poll interval` ≈ 35 minutes after a missed slot, versus potentially many hours before (accumulator's dropped 11:00 slot wasn't going to be caught by anything until its own next scheduled attempt at 18:00, 7 hours later).
- **Known tradeoff, accepted deliberately**: if GitHub's own cron delivery is just running late (not truly dropped) by more than `GRACE_MIN`, this mechanism will force-trigger a duplicate run that may then collide with GitHub's own delayed native trigger, risking two near-simultaneous posts for the same slot. Judged acceptable because (a) the downstream scripts already have their own duplicate-content guards for the exact-content case (title-similarity de-dupe in `agent_sports_blog.py`, brand cooldown state in `agent_telegram_offers.py`), and (b) "occasionally posts twice" is a much better failure mode than "silently posts zero times for hours," which is what the user actually reported.
- **YAML gotcha hit again while writing the bash inline in this same file**: same class of bug as the `transfer_news.yml` window-check step earlier the same day (colon-space inside an unquoted-by-YAML `run:` scalar breaks parsing) — avoided this time by writing the messages without `": "` from the start and validating with `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('file.yml'))"` before considering the edit done, per that entry's own stated lesson.
- **Tested the date-arithmetic logic locally before trusting it in CI**: macOS ships BSD `date` (no `-d` flag) and bash 3.2 (no working `declare -A` in the default `/bin/bash`), neither of which matches the `ubuntu-latest` runner this actually executes on. Installed `coreutils` (`brew install coreutils`, giving `gdate` with GNU semantics) and ran the extracted bash snippet under `/opt/homebrew/bin/bash` (a real bash 5.x) to verify both the "slot due today" and "fall back to yesterday's last slot" branches against the actual incident timestamps before committing. **Lesson: never trust GNU-`date`-flavored bash written for a Linux Actions runner just because it looks right — the local dev machine's own `bash`/`date` are frequently different enough (BSD vs GNU, bash 3.2 vs 5.x) to silently no-op or error on logic that would work fine in CI, so a quick local verification with GNU-compatible tools catches that before it ships.**
- **Pattern worth repeating for any future "why did nothing happen" report**: always distinguish "a workflow ran and failed" from "a workflow never ran" before reaching for the existing failure-retry tooling — `gh run list --workflow=X --limit N` showing a gap in the timeline (not a failure entry, an *absence*) is the tell, and neither of this repo's existing safety nets as they stood before this fix could see that distinction at all.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-28, later same day) — accumulator posts have been silently empty for days: Firecrawl ran out of credits, and its only scraper has no working free-tier fallback
- Follow-up investigation from the missed-schedule fix above. After manually re-triggering `accumulator_post.yml`, the run "succeeded" (workflow-level green) but still posted nothing — `✗ Only 0/5 qualifying real picks found`. Checked every accumulator run back to 2026-07-26 and found the same message every single time — this has been silently broken for at least 2 days, completely independent of the scheduling bug fixed above.
- **Two separate data-quality problems, traced to their actual source rather than patched at the point they were noticed**:
  1. `data/predictions.json` (141 real matches from predictz.com/forebet.com, real competition names) — every single entry has `confidence: null`. `agent_accumulator_post.py::gather_candidates()` requires a truthy `confidence` to consider a pick, so this source has structurally never contributed a candidate.
  2. `data/tips.json`'s only currently-live source (FreeSuperTips) hardcodes fake `odds: "1.80"` and `conf: 68` for literally every match, plus a generic `"league": "Football · FreeSuperTips"` label instead of the real competition (`agent_scrape_tips.py` line ~494) — checked the other two tip sources in that same file (PredictZ, Forebet) and found they *also* hardcode constant odds/confidence (`"odds": "1.85"`, `conf` fixed at 72/68/62 by home/away/draw direction only, never per-match). None of `agent_scrape_tips.py`'s three sources have ever produced a genuinely real, per-match attributed price — this predates the current outage and is a separate, deeper issue not addressed in this session (flagged here, not fixed, since fixing it means rewriting three scrapers to extract real per-match odds, out of scope for a same-day fix and not what was reported).
- **Root cause of problem #1, and why it can't be trivially fixed**: `data/predictions.json`'s `scrape_log` showed `"All scrapers failed ... firecrawl: HTTP Error 402: Payment Required"` on every page, every source, as of 2026-07-26T14:26 — the Firecrawl account is out of credits. `update_predictions.py` (repo root, generates `predictions.json`) calls Firecrawl + Apify **exclusively in parallel, with zero free fallback** — a direct violation of the CLAUDE.md "Multi-Source Scraping, Firecrawl-Last" standing rule, which the other three live scrapers (`agent_multi_scrape.py`, `agent_scrape_tips.py`, `agent_firecrawl_odds.py`) were already migrated to on 2026-07-24 but this one apparently never was.
- **Attempted the obvious fix, tested it live, and reverted it because it doesn't actually work**: swapped `update_predictions.py`'s `scrape()` to call `agents/python/utils/free_scrape.py` (the same trafilatura→Jina→Firecrawl helper the other three scrapers use). Confirmed live that trafilatura successfully fetches predictz.com (2563 chars, real content, real fractional odds visible to a human) — so the *fetch* problem is solved. But `update_predictions.py`'s parser (`MATCH_LINK`, `COMP_HEAD`, `FANDUEL` regexes) all match Firecrawl-specific markdown link syntax (`[TeamA v TeamB](https://predictz.com/predictions/.../123/`, `[1.85](https://predictz.com/fanduel/...`) — tested both trafilatura (`output_format='markdown'` included) and Jina Reader directly against the live page and **neither preserves any `[text](url)` hyperlinks for team names, competition headers, or odds** on this specific site; both just return readable prose/plain text. The free fetch layer "succeeds" by character count (clearing `free_scrape.py`'s `min_chars` gate) even though its content is structurally unparseable by this specific regex-based parser — worse, this would have **permanently prevented Firecrawl from ever being tried again**, even after credits are restored, since the free layer now always "wins" the length check first. Reverted via `git checkout -- update_predictions.py` rather than ship a change that looks like a fix but silently keeps producing zero real predictions while also disabling the one thing that used to work. **Lesson: always test a scraper swap against the actual downstream parser's expectations, not just "does content come back" — a free-tier fallback that returns real content in the wrong shape for the existing regex parser is not a fix, and can be a regression if it starves the previously-working path of ever running again.**
- **What was actually fixed, safely, in `agent_accumulator_post.py`** (kept, committed): (1) `gather_candidates()` no longer requires `predictions.json`'s (structurally always-null) `confidence` field — derives a real, non-fabricated proxy from the match's own real odds where they exist (`min(90, round(100 / odds))`, standard market-implied probability, consistent with this file's own "never invent a leg" policy) so this source is ready to contribute the moment real odds start flowing again (Firecrawl credits restored, or a future parser rework). (2) Fixed `is_major_league()`'s bare `"premier league"` keyword incorrectly matching Russia/Kazakhstan/Canada's own top flights (confirmed live in the current data) the same way `"serie a"` was already deliberately restricted to Italy/Brazil only. **Caught a real bug in my own first attempt at fix (2) by testing rather than trusting the diff**: the exclusion check ran *before* the African-league keyword check, so `"premier league"` being a substring of `"nigeria premier league"` too meant it also incorrectly rejected Nigeria/Kenya/Ghana/Egypt's premier leagues — moved the African-league check first and added a full test matrix (21 cases including the exact edge case that broke) before considering it done. Both changes are inert right now (0 candidates either way, since real odds still aren't flowing) but are correct and forward-compatible — no downside to shipping them now.
- **What remains genuinely unresolved, requires a decision only the account owner can make**: the accumulator (and any other feature depending on `data/predictions.json`) will keep finding 0 real qualifying picks until either (a) Firecrawl billing/credits are restored (`https://api.firecrawl.dev` account, not something fixable from inside the repo), or (b) `update_predictions.py`'s parser is rewritten to work off free-tier plain-text output instead of Firecrawl-specific markdown links — a genuinely bigger job (8 separate page-format parsers, `predictz.com` ×5 pages + `forebet.com` ×3 pages, each would need re-deriving match/competition/odds associations from prose instead of explicit hrefs) that risks shipping wrong data if rushed, not attempted in this session.
- **Pattern worth repeating**: when a "nothing is posting" report leads to a workflow that reports success but produces no output, keep pulling the thread past "the trigger fired" — a green checkmark at the workflow level says nothing about whether the underlying content pipeline actually found real data to post. This is the third distinct blind spot found in one afternoon (missed schedule trigger, this data-pipeline silence, and the earlier `AIProvidersExhausted` propagation issue) where "the job ran and reported success" was not evidence that the intended outcome (a real social post) actually happened.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-28, later same day) — found and fixed the actual cause of running out of Firecrawl credits in the first week: two scripts calling Firecrawl exclusively, one of them 96 times/day
- Direct follow-up to the "why is the accumulator empty" investigation above. User asked to (1) find a genuinely free/open-source, non-credit-metered odds source instead of depending on Firecrawl, and (2) get Firecrawl credit usage under control given the account ran out in its first week.
- **Audited every workflow that references `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY`** (`grep` across `.github/workflows/*.yml`) rather than guessing, and checked each underlying script's actual scrape call, not just its filename/docstring. Found two genuine violations of the CLAUDE.md "Multi-Source Scraping, Firecrawl-Last" standing rule — both calling Firecrawl **exclusively**, zero free-tier fallback:
  1. **`update_predictions.py`** (repo root, `update_predictions.yml`) — already found while investigating the accumulator (see entry above): Firecrawl + Apify only, 8 URLs (5 predictz.com pages + 3 forebet.com pages) **every 15 minutes, 24/7** = up to 96 runs/day × 8 URLs = the single largest Firecrawl consumer in the entire repo by a wide margin. This alone is almost certainly what exhausted a first-week credit allocation.
  2. **`update_countries.py`** (repo root, `update_countries_live.yml`, every 5 hours) — `fc_scrape()` called the Firecrawl CLI directly for all 16 bookmaker promo pages every run, no free fallback at all, despite its own docstring literally saying "Uses Firecrawl CLI... to scrape live bonus offers."
- **`update_countries.py` — fixed cleanly, this one actually works**: swapped `fc_scrape()` to call `agents/python/utils/free_scrape.py` (same trafilatura→Jina Reader→Firecrawl-last-resort helper already proven in 3 other scrapers). Unlike `update_predictions.py`'s parser, `extract_bonus()` here is plain regex over whatever text comes back — completely source-format-agnostic, so there was no link-syntax incompatibility to worry about. Verified live via direct `curl` to `r.jina.ai` (bypassing this dev sandbox's own rate-limited outbound IP, see below) that Jina Reader returns real, extractable bonus text for a real bookmaker promo page (Betika Kenya: 23,493 chars, "10% Cashia Bonus Promotion" clearly present) — confirmed trafilatura alone often returns *nothing* for these pages (tested live: 9,429 raw HTML chars downloaded, 0 chars extracted) since promo/marketing pages aren't the article-style content trafilatura's readability heuristics are tuned for, so Jina Reader is what actually carries this source most of the time, not trafilatura. Removed the now-fully-unused `subprocess`/`FC_DIR` plumbing that existed only for the old Firecrawl-CLI call.
- **`update_predictions.py` — could NOT be migrated the same way, reverted an attempted fix (see entry above), so the credit-management fix here is different**: since its regex parser (`MATCH_LINK`, `COMP_HEAD`, `FANDUEL`) needs Firecrawl-specific `[text](url)` markdown link syntax that neither trafilatura nor Jina Reader preserve for predictz.com/forebet.com (confirmed live, both ways, in the entry above), a source swap isn't viable without a genuinely bigger parser rewrite. Instead, cut `update_predictions.yml`'s schedule from every-15-minutes (96 runs/day) to **4x/day** (05:00, 10:00, 16:00, 22:00 UTC — timed just ahead of `agent_accumulator_post.py`'s 11:00/18:00 UTC posting slots, its only time-sensitive consumer; `agent_match_post.py`'s use of this data is on-demand/manual, not schedule-sensitive). This is a ~24x reduction in Firecrawl exposure from this one workflow with zero code risk, while every real consumer still gets fresh-enough data.
- **On "genuinely free, not-API/not-credit-dependent odds" — tested three realistic free-scraping paths live before concluding any of them are currently reliable enough to replace Firecrawl for *real per-match odds* specifically** (as opposed to bonus text, which the `update_countries.py` fix above handles fine): (1) OddsPortal (`oddsportal.com/matches/football/...`) is a fully client-side-rendered SPA — direct `curl` to Jina Reader returned 31,947 chars, but every byte of it was static shell (nav menu, cookie banner, footer); zero match rows or odds anywhere, confirmed by grepping for team-name/dash patterns. (2) Sofascore returned a near-empty 167-char response via Jina — consistent with the already-documented 2026-07-24 finding that Sofascore Cloudflare-blocks the Jina Reader proxy specifically. (3) PredictZ (the one source that *does* have real, freely-fetchable content — 2,563 chars via trafilatura, real team names and W/D/L form data) does **not** reliably expose real match-winner odds as visible text either way — the "1/X/2" odds buttons are click-to-add UI elements populated by JS after load; trafilatura only surfaced fraction odds for 3 "SUPER BOOST" *promotional* bet-builder blocks (not regular match-winner picks), and Jina Reader's extraction of the same page showed zero fraction-odds text at all. **Conclusion, stated plainly rather than shipping something unreliable**: none of the three realistic free-scraping targets for *real-time football match-winner odds specifically* are currently reliable without either Firecrawl's actual headless-browser JS rendering (which `free_scrape.py`'s trafilatura/Jina layers don't replicate) or a paid odds API (e.g. the-odds-api.com's free tier — genuinely free, no card, but still a third-party API with a key, which is a different tradeoff the user would need to explicitly choose, not something to add unilaterally). This is a real, currently-unclosed gap — flagged here rather than papered over.
- **Local dev sandbox caveat, worth remembering for future sessions doing live scraper testing here**: `r.jina.ai`'s free anonymous tier IP-rate-limits aggressively — after roughly 4-5 requests in quick succession from this exact sandbox, every subsequent call 403's for a while regardless of target URL (confirmed: predictz.com, oddsportal.com, sofascore.com, betika.com, sportybet.com all independently 403'd after the first few calls of this session, while the *first* call to each of the first two succeeded fine via direct `curl`). This is IP-based, not URL-based, and doesn't reproduce in production since GitHub Actions runners get a fresh IP per run — already documented once on 2026-07-24, re-confirmed here. **When verifying a Jina-Reader-dependent fix locally, get your strongest single piece of evidence early (first 1-2 calls) and trust it once obtained — don't keep re-testing through an already-triggered rate limit and mistake "sandbox is rate-limited" for "the fix doesn't work."**

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-28, later same day) — transfer news found "no fresh news" for 30 out of 31 checks (2.5+ hours) because Google News RSS attribution was thrown away instead of read
- User reported still no Telegram posts from the transfer-news bot after the earlier Playwright/window-gate fixes. Pulled the live in-progress run's log and found every single 5-minute cycle (30/31 over 2h38m) logging `⚠ No fresh news found for transfers (all items exceed freshness window) — skipping` — a genuine "no data" condition, not a crash, so nothing in the earlier session's fixes could have caught this.
- **Traced the actual cause layer by layer rather than guessing**: `fetch_category("transfers")` uses 3 layers (DuckDuckGo search, Google News RSS, site RSS). A prior session (2026-07-27, commit `77c767484`) had added a strict 6-outlet allowlist (`TRANSFER_ALLOWED_SOURCE_TERMS`: bbc/sky sports/espn/talksport/athletic/romano) for attribution quality, and disabled the Google News RSS layer entirely for this category on the stated reasoning that "Google News RSS hardcodes its item source to the literal string 'Google News'" and would post an unattributable source. Checked live: (1) DuckDuckGo's raw results for transfer queries were dominated by non-allowlisted aggregators (HITC, MSN, Yahoo Sports, TEAMtalk) — all correctly filtered out by the allowlist, leaving almost nothing. (2) The one dedicated site RSS feed (`BBC Transfers Dedicated`) was itself stale — `curl`'d directly, confirmed only 1 `<item>` in the whole feed, dated 39+ hours old (BBC's dedicated transfers feed just isn't updated often). (3) Google News RSS — the layer that was disabled — was checked directly via `curl` and found to carry a real per-item `<source url="...">` element for every article (`Sky Sports`, `BBC`, `talkSPORT`, `ESPN`, `Football365`, ...), contradicting the assumption that started this whole chain: the code just never read that element, always hardcoding `"Google News"` regardless of what the feed actually said.
- **Fix**: `_parse_rss()` gained a `use_item_source: bool = False` parameter — when true, reads each item's own `<source>` element instead of the single fixed label every other (single-outlet) RSS feed passes in. `_google_news_search()` now calls it with `use_item_source=True`. Re-enabled the Google News RSS layer for `"transfers"` (previously the only category it was skipped for) now that its items carry real, checkable attribution — `_is_allowed_transfer_source()` still filters every item from every layer exactly as before, so the allowlist itself (the actual attribution-quality guarantee) is completely unchanged; only the previously-wasted Google News layer got switched back on with correct sourcing.
- **Verified live, not just by reading the diff**: `fetch_category("transfers")` went from 0 items to **21 real items**, newest 1.9 hours old, sources confirmed to be exactly `{'Sky Sports', 'ESPN', 'BBC'}` — no leakage of non-allowlisted outlets. Also spot-checked `football`/`sportnews` categories (102/100 items respectively) to confirm the change doesn't regress anything for categories that were already working — this is a strict improvement for them too, since their Google News items now carry real per-article attribution instead of a blanket "Google News" label.
- **Pattern worth repeating**: when a fix from an earlier session disables a whole data source "because it can't provide real attribution," verify that premise directly against the live feed before accepting it as still true — the underlying API/feed can carry more structured data than the code that consumes it actually reads, and "we can't use X for reason Y" is worth re-checking with a raw `curl`/`grep` rather than trusting a code comment, especially once that comment is itself old enough to have been reasoned about under different information.

### SEO Learnings / Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-30) — full schema markup audit: the 2026-07-19 JSON-LD escaping fix only ever landed in one of eleven generators
- User asked for a thorough schema-markup SEO audit plus an llms.txt. llms.txt already existed (`/llms.txt`, 12KB, last updated 2026-07-28, 23 countries + verified bonuses + AI-citation FAQ block) and needed no changes — audit effort went entirely into schema.
- **Confirmed via the `seo-schema` skill's own deprecation reference** (`~/.claude/skills/seo-schema/references/deprecated-types-2024-2026.md`) two things this codebase already had exposure to: FAQPage rich results have been restricted to government/health sites only since August 2023 (every non-gov/health FAQPage on the site — which is all of them — gets zero Google SERP rich-result benefit, though it's still legitimate for Bing and for AI/LLM citation legibility, so **not removed**, just noted as no-SERP-effect); HowTo rich results were removed entirely in September 2023 (same "keep for AI legibility, no SERP benefit" call applied to the one HowTo block in `gen_payment_pages.py`). Neither is "broken" schema, just non-beneficial for classic Google rich snippets — flagged to the user rather than stripped, per the skill's own guidance to treat both as Info-level, not Critical.
- **The actual bug, and the one that mattered**: CLAUDE.md's SEO Health table claims the 2026-07-19 "manual quote-escaping breaks JSON-LD" bug was fixed globally via `json.dumps()`. It was fixed **only in `gen_blog_post_pages.py`**. Grepped every `gen_*.py`/`generate_*.py` for `json.dumps` and found zero hits in the other ten page generators (`gen_all_cities.py`, `gen_bonus_pages.py`, `gen_bookmaker_country_pages.py`, `gen_city_pages.py`, `gen_guide_pages.py`, `gen_payment_country_pages.py`, `gen_payment_pages.py`, `gen_sport_country_pages.py`, `generate_country_pages.py`, and `gen_bk_reviews.py` which had no FAQ schema at all — see below). Four of those (`gen_bonus_pages.py`, `gen_guide_pages.py`, `gen_payment_pages.py`, and — inside `faq_schema_json()` — `generate_country_pages.py`) used the exact same broken `.replace('"', '\\"')` pattern the 2026-07-19 fix was supposed to have eliminated everywhere (misses newlines/backslashes, double-escapes on re-runs). Five more (`gen_all_cities.py`, `gen_bookmaker_country_pages.py`, `gen_city_pages.py`, `gen_payment_country_pages.py`, `gen_sport_country_pages.py`) had **zero escaping at all** — raw f-string interpolation straight into a JSON string context. None of these had actually produced invalid JSON yet in the live pages (confirmed via `scripts/validate_site.py` CHECK 3 passing both before and after), because the interpolated values (city/country/bookmaker/payment-method names) happen to be short controlled proper nouns unlikely to contain a literal `"` — but the vulnerability was real and matched the documented severity class exactly; it was a matter of when, not if, an FAQ answer sentence containing a quoted phrase would silently break structured data on however many of the ~200+ pages those ten generators produce.
- **Fix applied uniformly**: added `import json` to all ten files and rebuilt every FAQ/HowTo JSON-LD node via `json.dumps({...})` (a real dict, not manual string formatting) instead of hand-built `'{{"@type":...}}'.format(...)` or bare f-strings. `gen_city_pages.py` needed restructuring since its FAQPage block was inlined directly into the page-template f-string rather than built as a separate `faq_schema` variable first — extracted a `faq_items`/`faq_schema` pair matching the pattern every other generator already used, then referenced `{faq_schema}` in the template. Also defensively wrapped `WebPage.name`/`description`/`BreadcrumbList` item names in `json.dumps()` in `gen_city_pages.py` and `generate_country_pages.py` while already in there (lower risk than FAQ prose, but free to fix given the surrounding edit).
- **Separate real bug found and fixed while in `gen_bk_reviews.py`**: `itemReviewed.@type` was `"LocalBusiness"` for online-only bookmaker reviews (Bet9ja, SportyBet, etc.) — LocalBusiness expects a physical `address`/`geo` that these entities don't have, so this would surface as a "missing required field" warning in Google's Rich Results Test. The sibling generator `gen_bookmaker_country_pages.py` already had this right (`"@type": "Organization"`) for the exact same kind of entity — the two review-page generators had silently drifted apart. Fixed to `Organization`, matching the correct sibling. Also found `gen_bk_reviews.py` renders a fully visible on-page FAQ section (`<h2>Frequently Asked Questions — {name}</h2>`) with **no matching FAQPage schema at all** — every other page type on the site pairs visible FAQ content with FAQPage JSON-LD; this one generator was missed. Added the matching `FAQPage` node.
- **Homepage (`index.html`, hand-maintained, no generator) had two separate, un-linked JSON-LD entities describing the same real-world organization**: one `Organization` node (`@id: #organization`, in the main `@graph`, with logo/sameAs/areaServed/contactPoint) and a second, separate `<script>` block declaring `@type: "OnlineBusiness"` (`@id: #localbusiness`) with `openingHours`, `priceRange`, and `currenciesAccepted` — verified via WebFetch against schema.org's own type definition that **`OnlineBusiness` is a real, valid schema.org type** (inherits `Thing > Organization > OnlineBusiness`), but none of those three properties are defined anywhere in its inheritance chain; they're LocalBusiness-only vocabulary silently grafted onto a type that can't use them. Fixed by merging: the main `Organization` node's `@type` became `["Organization", "OnlineBusiness"]` (keeps the more specific typing Google/AI can use, no vocabulary mismatch), the second orphaned block was deleted, and its one genuinely useful unique property (`image`) was folded into the surviving node. Verified both remaining JSON-LD blocks re-parse with `json.loads()` after the edit.
- **Added `ItemList` schema to `bookmakers/index.html`** (the 7-bookmaker review listing/hub page) — it previously had `WebPage` + `BreadcrumbList` only despite being a literal static list page, a natural fit for `ItemList` that was simply missing.
- **`scripts/validate_site.py` CHECK 3 already existed and already parses every `index.html` site-wide for JSON-LD validity** (not just blog posts) — it's the correct permanent guard for this whole bug class and needed no new check, only a comment correction (it previously implied the 2026-07-19 fix was global; corrected to document that it wasn't, and now is). Ran it after all fixes: `0 errors` across all 4 checks.
- **Deliberately did NOT force-regenerate the ~81 pre-existing city pages `gen_all_cities.py` produces**, even though its source now has the fix — that generator explicitly skips existing output files with a code comment stating this is to "preserve hand-crafted pages like Lagos, Nairobi." Since `validate_site.py` confirmed none of those existing pages are currently broken (the vulnerability was latent, never triggered), and blindly force-overwriting risked clobbering intentional hand-customization the original author explicitly protected against, left them as-is — the source fix protects every future run of that generator, which is the part that actually matters going forward.
- **Near-miss, caught before committing: ran all ten fixed generators against the live tree and it silently reverted a large amount of unrelated, already-shipped production content.** First instinct after fixing the ten `.py` source files was to re-run every one of them to actually push the fix into the live HTML (`gen_bookmaker_country_pages.py`, `gen_payment_country_pages.py`, `gen_sport_country_pages.py`, `generate_country_pages.py`, plus the unconditional-overwrite ones: `gen_bonus_pages.py`, `gen_guide_pages.py`, `gen_payment_pages.py`, `gen_bk_reviews.py`, `gen_city_pages.py` — ~200 files written). `git diff` on the result showed each touched page losing real, already-live improvements that don't exist anywhere in the checked-out generator source: the GA `gtag()` snippet, `<link rel="preload">`/`preconnect` hints, a CWV `content-visibility` CSS block, cache-busted asset URLs (`?v=10`), `fetchpriority="high"` on the logo image, a consolidated favicon `<link>` (`/assets/favicon.svg`) replacing four separate ICO/PNG favicon tags, normalized `meta name="author"`, shortened `<title>`/WebPage `name` text, and a removed `hreflang="x-default"` link. Crucially: spot-checking `bookmakers/bet9ja/index.html`'s **committed** (`git show HEAD:`) content showed `itemReviewed.@type` was *already* `"Organization"`, not `"LocalBusiness"` — the exact bug this session set out to fix in `gen_bk_reviews.py` had **already been fixed live**, independently of the stale generator source, for 6 of 7 bookmaker pages (only FAQPage schema was actually still missing on those 6 — `bet9ja` itself already had both fixes live). The `.py` generators in this repo are not a reliable source of truth for already-shipped pages; some live content has clearly been hand-patched or produced by a since-diverged pipeline without the `.py` source ever being updated to match.
- **Recovery**: `git reset` to unstage, then `git checkout --` on all ~198 regenerated-but-not-hand-edited files to restore the live/committed versions exactly, keeping only the two files edited surgically with the `Edit` tool (`index.html`, `bookmakers/index.html` — confirmed via `git diff` to contain *only* the intended schema change, nothing else). For the real remaining live gap (6 bookmaker pages missing `FAQPage` schema despite visible on-page FAQ content), wrote a small standalone script (not a re-run of `gen_bk_reviews.py`) that regex-extracts each page's actual live `.faq-item` HTML, builds a `FAQPage` node via `json.dumps()`, and splices it into the existing `@graph` array via a single, validated string replacement — parsed the result with `json.loads()` before writing, so no file could be left broken. Diffed every touched file afterward to confirm each change was minimal and additive only. Final `git status` after cleanup: 20 meaningful files changed (11 `.py` sources + 6 surgically-patched bookmaker pages + `bookmakers/index.html` + `index.html` + `validate_site.py` comment + this file), versus ~213 if the blanket regeneration had shipped.
- **Pattern worth repeating (two, from the same near-miss)**: (1) when CLAUDE.md documents a bug as "fixed" with a named root-cause file, don't assume the fix was applied everywhere the same bug pattern could occur — grep for the actual fix signature (here, `json.dumps`) across every file that plausibly shares the vulnerable code shape, not just the one file the standing-rule entry names. (2) **Never trust a page generator's source as the current source of truth for already-deployed output without diffing a regenerated sample first** — a generator that hasn't been run in a while can be stale relative to production in ways a source-code read alone won't reveal (hand patches, a since-abandoned newer pipeline, manual SEO/perf tweaks applied directly to HTML). Fixing the generator source is still correct and worth doing (protects whatever *does* eventually re-run it), but pushing that fix into already-live pages requires a `git diff` sanity check on at least one regenerated file before trusting a bulk re-run — bulk-regenerating first and inspecting after is how ~200 files of real, already-shipped SEO/performance work almost got silently discarded under the banner of "making schema fully SEO ready."

### SEO Learnings (2026-07-30, same day follow-up) — closing out the schema audit: verified no live escaping defect remains, found 3 more real gaps outside the main generators
- User asked "is everything fixed or do you need to do more" after the above session. Rather than re-doing the bulk regeneration that was already reverted, did two things: (1) verified there is genuinely nothing left to patch for the JSON-LD escaping issue on already-live pages, (2) audited the page types that don't come from any of the 11 `gen_*.py`/`generate_*.py` generators.
- **On (1) — the escaping fix has no live artifact to patch, by construction**: `scripts/validate_site.py` CHECK 3 already confirms every live JSON-LD block parses as valid JSON. A string that parses as valid JSON is, by definition, already correctly escaped for its current content — there is nothing to "fix" in output that isn't broken. The source-level fix's only real value is protecting the *next* run of these generators against *future* data containing a quote character; it can't retroactively change already-valid content. Also spot-checked site-wide for the two other live-content defects found earlier in the session (`grep -rl '"@type": "LocalBusiness"'` → zero hits anywhere; `FAQPage` presence sampled across bookmaker×country, payment×country, sport×country, country, bonus, guide, and city pages → present on every one checked) — confirms those fixes are complete everywhere, not just on the 6 bookmaker pages patched directly.
- **On (2) — three real, previously-unaudited gaps found and fixed**:
  1. `leagues/index.html` (live football scores/schedules hub) and `tools/index.html` (odds/parlay calculator hub) had **zero JSON-LD** at all — no `WebPage`, no `BreadcrumbList`, nothing. Added the standard `WebPage` + `BreadcrumbList` pattern used everywhere else on the site (`isPartOf: {"@id": "https://sifufinds.com/#website"}`); `tools/index.html` also got an `ItemList` since it's a literal 2-item static listing page, same reasoning as the earlier `bookmakers/index.html` fix.
  2. `press/index.html` used `"@type": "NewsArticle"` for a static, evergreen press/media-kit page (key stats, editorial contacts, downloadable assets) — a genuine type mismatch, since NewsArticle rich results are meant for actual time-sensitive journalism, not an evergreen about-style page. Replaced with `WebPage` + `BreadcrumbList`, matching the pattern already used correctly by `about/`, `contact/`, `privacy/`, `terms/`, and linked it into the site's entity graph via `isPartOf`/`about` referencing the existing `#website`/`#organization` `@id`s (this page previously had no `@id`/`isPartOf` linkage at all, sitting completely disconnected from the rest of the site's structured data).
  3. Spot-checked `casino/`, `tables/`, `about/`, `contact/`, `privacy/`, `terms/`, `blog/`, `countries/`, `betting/`, `bonuses/`, `guides/` hub pages — all already correctly typed (`WebPage`/`AboutPage`/`ContactPage`/`Blog`/`ItemList` + `BreadcrumbList`, `about/` additionally has well-formed `Organization`+`Person` E-E-A-T nodes for Sifu Kai). No further gaps found.
- **Recurring operational hazard, hit twice in one session**: this repo has active background automation writing directly into the working tree *during* an interactive session (not just scheduled GitHub Actions) — first `.htaccess`/`agents/python/utils/affiliate_links.py` (new brand additions), then mid-session again `blog/banners-data.js`/`blog/banners.json`/`blog/index.html` (a sponsored-banner injection system adding `.article-inline-banner-raw` CSS and banner data). Neither set of changes was requested or touched by this session's work. Handled by excluding them from every `git add` (staged explicitly by filename throughout, never `git add -A`), and — when they started blocking a `git pull --no-rebase` merge with "your local changes would be overwritten" — `git stash push -u -m "..." -- <specific files>` rather than discarding them, preserving that work for whoever/whatever owns it. **Lesson: on this specific repo, always check `git status` immediately before `git add`/`git commit`/`git pull`, even seconds after the last check — local file state can change between tool calls here, not just between sessions.**
- **Also hit**: `git push` was rejected twice in this session (`fetch first`) because this repo's automated pipelines (live odds, transfer news, leagues, doctor auto-heal) commit and push directly to `main` roughly every few minutes. Resolved both times with a plain `git fetch` + `git pull origin main --no-rebase` (clean merge both times, no real conflicts since the automation and this session's schema edits never touched the same lines) followed by `git push`. **On this repo specifically, always fetch+merge immediately before pushing, even if the working session was short** — origin moves fast enough that a push can go stale within minutes.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-30) — transfer news was attaching wrong/random player photos because it searched by extracted name instead of using the source article's own image
- User reported transfer-news posts on Telegram kept showing random/wrong images and asked to stop inventing images entirely — use whatever image the source article itself carries.
- **Root cause**: `agent_transfer_post.py`'s main flow extracted a player name from the generated article via an LLM fact-extraction pass, then called `utils/player_photo.py::find_player_image()` — a speculative DuckDuckGo-image-search-by-name / Wikipedia lookup with no connection to the actual source story. Common football surnames, players who share a name, or an LLM extracting a slightly-off name all produced a photo of the wrong person with high confidence and no way to catch it after the fact. The raw AI-outage fallback path (`find_raw_fallback_photo()`) had the same problem one layer worse: it regex-extracted name-*shaped* phrases from a raw headline with no LLM at all, then ran the same speculative search.
- **The actual fix, not a smaller patch on the search itself**: stopped searching for a photo by name at all. `utils/news_fetcher.py`'s `NewsItem` gained a real `image` field — populated from DuckDuckGo News search results (which already carry a genuine per-article thumbnail in their raw `image` key, previously discarded) and from RSS `<media:thumbnail>`/`<media:content>`/`<enclosure>` tags (confirmed live: BBC's RSS feed items carry a real `<media:thumbnail url="https://ichef.bbci.co.uk/...">` per story). `agent_sports_blog.py::generate_post()` now carries the raw candidate news items (title/url/image/source) through as `post["_source_items"]`. `agent_transfer_post.py`'s new `find_source_image(post, facts)` matches the LLM-extracted player/club facts against each source item's *title* (keyword overlap, not a fresh search) to find which specific story the article is actually about, then uses **that item's own image** — never a name-based lookup. `find_raw_fallback_photo(item)` is even simpler: the raw headline *is* the source item, so it just uses `item["image"]` directly, no extraction step needed at all.
- **Guarded against a real gotcha found by testing, not by reading the code**: some RSS thumbnails are generic section-branding graphics reused across unrelated stories (Sky Sports "Paper Talk", BBC "branded_*" cards — the exact same failure mode already documented and fixed for the *blog feature-image* pipeline on 2026-07-27). Reused the existing `_looks_like_branded_thumbnail_url()` / `_looks_like_news_column_graphic()` guards from `utils/player_photo.py` (rather than duplicating the logic) inside a new `_usable_source_image()` check that both `find_source_image()` and `find_raw_fallback_photo()` call. Verified live: the one image the current feed actually offered ("All done deals in July 2026", a monthly round-up piece) was correctly rejected by the "done deal" title hint even though its URL pattern looked fine — a generic round-up thumbnail is exactly the kind of non-story-specific image this guard exists to catch.
- **When no source image survives all the guards (or none exists for that specific story), the fallback is the existing per-post branded graphic — never another speculative search.** This matches the "no photo beats a wrong photo" principle already established elsewhere in this codebase for the blog feature-image pipeline, now applied consistently to the Telegram/Facebook social-bulletin image too.
- **`post["_source_items"]` is stripped before `save_posts()`** — it's only needed transiently to pick the image right after `generate_post()` returns, and would otherwise permanently bloat `blog/posts.json` (which every visitor's browser fetches) with title/url/image data for up to 8 candidate stories per post, forever.
- **Verified with real unit tests, not just by reading the diff**: confirmed `find_source_image()` picks the *correct* one of two candidate stories by matching extracted facts against titles (not just "the first" or "the freshest"), confirmed it returns `(None, None)` rather than forcing a wrong match when no candidate's title overlaps the facts at all, and confirmed the branded-thumbnail/news-column guards actually reject the known-bad URL/title patterns.
- **Also removed as dead code**: the entire name-candidate-extraction + Wikipedia-person-verification chain that only existed to support the old speculative-search fallback (`_extract_name_candidates`, `_KNOWN_CLUBS_AND_COMPETITIONS`, `_wikipedia_confirms_person`, etc.) — confirmed via `grep` these had no other call sites before deleting.
- **Auto-deploy pipeline interference mid-session, again**: the `news_fetcher.py` half of this fix got swept into an unrelated `deploy: 2026-07-30 08:23 UTC` auto-commit (alongside `data/live.json`/`data/health.json` updates from other agents) before this session ran its own `git commit` — same phenomenon already documented on 2026-07-24 and 2026-07-30 earlier in this file. Verified the auto-committed version was the complete, correct edit (`git show HEAD:path | grep` for the new symbols, matching line count) before committing the remaining two files (`agent_transfer_post.py`, `agent_sports_blog.py`) separately rather than assuming something was lost.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-30, later same day) — "no post on Twitter for 9 hrs": found two independent real causes, one a silent-failure blind spot none of the retry safety nets could see

- User reported no X (Twitter) posts for ~9 hours. Checked `gh run list --workflow=twitter_posts.yml` rather than assuming — every recent run showed `completed success`, so the workflow-level view alone looked fine. Dug into the actual gap timeline: last run before the reported gap was `21:45 UTC 2026-07-29` (a delayed `0 21 * * *` slot), then **nothing at all** until a `workflow_dispatch` at `09:46 UTC 2026-07-30` — that dispatch is `retry_failed.yml`'s own "missed schedule" catch-up firing (confirmed: it already lists `twitter_posts.yml` with hours `9 13 17 21`), followed by the *actual* delayed `0 9 * * *` cron finally firing at `11:08 UTC`, over 2 hours late. So the existing missed-schedule safety net (added 2026-07-28, see entry above) did work exactly as designed here — but it couldn't shrink the gap below roughly 9-13 hours because the schedule itself has a **built-in 12-hour dead zone every night** (`21 * * *` → `9 * * *` next day, with nothing in between). `GRACE_MIN=20` + a 15-min poll only bounds *detection* of a missed slot; it can't fill a gap the cron schedule itself leaves open by design.
- **Fix 1 (structural, permanent)**: added a `0 1 * * *` cron slot to `twitter_posts.yml` (now 5x/day: 01:00, 09:00, 13:00, 17:00, 21:00 UTC) and updated `retry_failed.yml`'s `SCHEDULES["twitter_posts.yml"]` from `"9 13 17 21"` to `"1 9 13 17 21"` to match, so the missed-schedule detector still checks the right slots. This caps the longest possible gap at 8h (`01→09`) even with zero GitHub-side delay — structurally impossible to reproduce a 9h+ silent gap again from schedule shape alone. Didn't jump to hourly/6x — the account cooldown/template-rotation logic doesn't need more frequent posting than this to stay fresh, and the goal was closing the specific dead zone, not maximizing volume.
- **Fix 2 (the one that actually matters more, and that the missed-schedule check is structurally blind to)**: `agents/python/agent_twitter_posts.py::_post_playwright()` clicked the Post button, waited 4s, then unconditionally printed `✓ Tweet posted via browser` and `return True` — **no verification the post actually landed.** X can return a normal-looking, non-crashing response for a click that didn't actually submit (duplicate-content rejection, rate limit, a stale/soft-expired session that still renders `x.com/home`) and this code had no way to notice. Because the workflow step would still exit 0, `retry_failed.yml`'s failure-check (`conclusion == "failure"`) and the missed-schedule check (`last run's createdAt` looks recent) would **both** see a healthy-looking workflow and never retry — a silent failure invisible to every existing safety net, a genuinely different blind spot than the "trigger never fired" class already documented on 2026-07-28.
- **The actual fix**: registered a `page.on("response", handler)` listener before navigating, capturing the `CreateTweet` GraphQL mutation's HTTP status + JSON body (X's real tweet-submission endpoint). After clicking Post and the existing 4s wait, check that a `CreateTweet` response was actually observed at all (raise if not — the click may not have submitted anything), then check `status == 200` and no `errors` array in the body (X returns 200 with a populated `errors` array for plenty of rejections, e.g. `"Status is a duplicate."`) — raise with the real X-provided error message otherwise. The existing `_post_playwright_sync()` wrapper already catches exceptions and returns `False`, which `run()` already turns into `log(..., "failed", ...)` + `sys.exit(1)` — so a genuinely-failed post now makes the *workflow* fail too, which is exactly the signal both existing retry mechanisms need to actually catch it and retry automatically. No changes needed to the retry infrastructure itself — the gap was purely that the poster script was lying about success.
- **Verified before committing, not just by reading the diff**: confirmed via `inspect.getsource(Page.on)` that Playwright's async API type signature explicitly accepts `Callable[..., Awaitable[None]]` (async handlers are supported, not just sync), and via `inspect.getsource(pyee.asyncio.AsyncIOEventEmitter._emit_run)` that a coroutine returned from an event handler is scheduled with `ensure_future` on the same loop — meaning the existing `await page.wait_for_timeout(4_000)` after the click already gives the handler ample time to run to completion before the result dict is read, no additional wait needed. Also validated both edited workflow YAML files with `python3 -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open(f))"` per the repo's own documented `run:`-string YAML gotcha (2026-07-28 entries above), and compiled the Python file with `python3 -m py_compile`. Did **not** trigger a real (non-dry-run) post to verify the new failure path live — doing so risks either double-posting against the real account rotation state or requires deliberately manufacturing a duplicate-content rejection against production, neither of which is worth the risk to test a change whose correctness is already established by the type/source inspection above; ran a `workflow_dispatch` with `dry_run=true` instead to confirm the YAML/job structure itself still runs cleanly post-edit.
- **Pattern worth repeating**: "the workflow succeeded" and "the intended real-world action actually happened" are different claims, and this is now the *second* distinct way this repo's automation has conflated them in one week (the first was the accumulator posting "success" while finding 0 real picks, 2026-07-28 entry above) — for any bot that drives a third-party UI (browser automation, not a real API), success must be verified against that third party's own response/confirmation signal, never inferred from "the click didn't throw."

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-30, immediate same-session follow-up) — the CreateTweet verification above then proved the poster had likely NEVER actually posted: root cause was the click on the Post button silently landing on nothing, not any content/scheduling issue

- Direct continuation of the entry above. User said "nothing has been posted, post something so I know it's working" — a stronger claim than the original "9 hrs", prompting a live, real (non-dry-run) `workflow_dispatch` test rather than trusting the fix from static analysis alone. First live attempt (offer mode, BetWinner) hit the brand-new `CreateTweet` verification's failure path: `No CreateTweet network response observed`.
- **Wrong first hypothesis, stated and then disproven rather than shipped**: assumed this was duplicate-content rejection, since `_build_offer_tweet()` produces byte-for-byte identical text every time for a given brand (fully static template, no dynamic content) and BetWinner's exact text had already been posted 2 days prior — X does reject exact-repeat text client-side in some cases. Tested this directly by forcing `mode=news` (blog-post text is unique per post/URL, structurally cannot be a duplicate) — **same exact failure**, ruling the hypothesis out immediately rather than leaving it as an unverified guess in a commit message.
- **Real root cause, found by adding diagnostics and reading the actual evidence instead of guessing again**: instrumented `_post_playwright()` to capture (a) every `/i/api/graphql/` response seen (not just CreateTweet) so a wrong-endpoint theory could be checked, (b) whether the compose textarea was still visible after the click (still-open = definitely not submitted), (c) the Post button's `aria-disabled` attribute before/after the click, (d) any toast/alert text. Live result: compose box still visible = `True`, zero new GraphQL calls fired as a result of the click (the only post-type calls were `ComposerJetfuelPreviewQuery` — link-preview-card generation that fires *while typing*, not from the click), button was not disabled. **The click produced literally zero effect of any kind.**
- **Second hypothesis, also tested and disproven rather than assumed**: the existing button locator (`page.locator('[data-testid="tweetButton"], [data-testid="tweetButtonInline"]').first`) resolves a combined CSS selector and takes `.first` in raw DOM order — not necessarily the instance actually wired to the inline compose box being typed into. Rewrote it to explicitly prefer `tweetButtonInline` when the inline compose path was used (confirmed via the existing `compose_opened` flag) rather than trusting DOM order. Re-tested live: log confirmed `Using post button: [data-testid="tweetButtonInline"]` (the theoretically-correct one) — **still zero effect, same diagnostics.** This ruled out "wrong button" cleanly rather than being left as an unverified assumption.
- **Actual root cause**: the code's own long-standing comment said `force=True bypasses the #layers overlay that intercepts pointer events on x.com` (present since an earlier session's commit `60e220003`, well before this one). That comment embeds a real misconception: Playwright's `force: true` only skips Playwright's own pre-click *actionability checks* (visibility, stability, "receives pointer events") — it does not change how the click is delivered. Under the hood it's still a coordinate-based `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` at the element's bounding-box center, and the real browser still hit-tests whatever DOM node is topmost at those screen coordinates at the moment of the click. If `#layers` (or anything else) is genuinely rendered on top of the button, `force: true` still lands the click on that overlay, not the button underneath — it never actually reaches the intended element. This is consistent with every symptom observed: no exception (Playwright itself has no way to know the click "landed on the wrong thing" — from its perspective the click succeeded), so the old code's blind `return True` after the click made this invisible for however long that comment/pattern has existed.
- **Fix**: switched the *primary* submission mechanism from clicking the Post button to X's native `Ctrl+Enter` / `Cmd+Enter` keyboard shortcut for submitting the compose box — a keyboard event has no click target to miss, sidestepping the overlay/hit-testing problem entirely rather than fighting it. Tries `Control+Enter` first, then `Meta+Enter` only if the first produced no `CreateTweet` response (checked via the same network-capture mechanism, so it's a real confirmation, not a timing guess) — verified live this matters: `Control+Enter` alone did nothing (correctly detected as no-response, loop advanced without submitting anything), `Meta+Enter` is what actually worked, consistent with the session-storage-state branch's Mac-flavored user agent. Kept the old click-based approach as a final fallback (now correctly picking the testid matching the actual compose surface, per the fix above) only for the case where neither keyboard binding works, rather than deleting a working-adjacent code path.
- **Verified with a real, non-dry-run post, not just a clean exit code**: `gh run view <id> --log` showed `Submitting via Control+Enter...` → `Submitting via Meta+Enter...` → `✓ Tweet posted via browser — CreateTweet confirmed (HTTP 200)`. This is the account's first *provably* real post of this session (every prior "success" in this bot's history, including all of 2026-07-30's earlier runs referenced in the entry above, could have been subject to the exact same silent click failure — there is no way to retroactively know how many of them actually landed, since the verification that would have caught it didn't exist until this session).
- **Pattern worth repeating, sharper version of the one in the entry above**: when browser automation "succeeds" with no visible error, that only means *no exception was thrown* — it says nothing about whether the intended DOM interaction actually reached its target. A `force=True` click is not a guarantee of delivery; it guarantees Playwright won't complain about *pre-click* conditions, but the browser's real hit-testing still applies at dispatch time. When a UI element might be covered by an overlay, prefer an input mechanism with no spatial target at all (keyboard shortcuts, form submission via Enter, programmatic `dispatchEvent` on the specific node) over trying to force a coordinate-based click through whatever is on top.
- **Also worth repeating**: don't ship the first plausible hypothesis. Two were tested and killed in this session (duplicate content, wrong button) before the real cause was found — each took one extra live round-trip, but each was verified against real evidence (a second live failure with different content ruled out #1; a diagnostic log line confirming the "fixed" button was actually selected, yet still failing, ruled out #2) rather than being assumed fixed after a plausible-looking code change.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-30, later same day) — transfer news was STILL sending mismatched images after the source-image fix, because the underlying stories feeding it were wrong, not the image-selection code

- User reported transfer-news images were *still* wrong even after the same-day `find_source_image()` fix (see entry above). Rather than re-patching image selection again, checked the actual live Telegram channel (`https://t.me/s/sifufinds` public preview, no bot token needed) against `blog/posts.json` and the live BBC transfers RSS feed to find what was really happening — the image-selection code itself turned out to be working correctly; the *inputs* it was being fed were the problem.
- **Real cause #1 — a BBC "rolling recap" item was being treated as breaking news.** `feeds.bbci.co.uk/sport/football/transfers/rss.xml`'s top (often only) item is a perpetually-republished tracker page titled "All done deals in July 2026" — BBC refreshes its `pubDate` on every update, so it always passes the 24h freshness window looking exactly like a fresh single story, but its actual content is a running list spanning the whole transfer window, including **years-old completed deals** (Ronaldo→Al Nassr from 2022, cited as one bullet). `generate_post("transfers")` wove this into an article ("July Transfer Frenzy: From Rodri Rumours to Grealish Saudi Moves") as if it were part of "the last 13 hours" of news, and `extract_transfer_facts()` then picked that Ronaldo bullet as the social bulletin's single `headline_fact` — producing a live Telegram post (`t.me/sifufinds/834`) captioned "Cristiano Ronaldo has switched from Manchester United to Al Nassr" but linking/imaged to the Rodri/Grealish article. The image itself was technically correct (it matched the post it was attached to) — the *caption* was the actual mismatch, which is what reads as "wrong image" to a viewer scanning the channel. BBC's daily gossip column (`"Football gossip: Rodri, Jones, Romero, Kolo Muani, Sangare, Bailey, Matsima"`, confirmed live via Google News RSS too) has the exact same failure shape: one item bundling many different players' rumours, breaking the single-headline/single-image assumption everywhere downstream.
- **The actual fix, at the fetch layer, not the image layer**: added `_is_rolling_recap()` + `_ROLLING_RECAP_TITLE_HINTS` to `utils/news_fetcher.py`, applied inside `fetch_category()` for every category (not just transfers) right after the existing transfer-source allowlist filter, so a bundling/tracker item can never enter the candidate pool `generate_post()` writes from in the first place. Deliberately narrower than the existing `player_photo.py::_looks_like_news_column_graphic()` (which matches bare `"done deal"` for its image-branding-detection purpose) — that would also match a genuine single confirmed-transfer headline like "Player X's move to Club Y is now a done deal," and dropping a real scoop from the whole feed is a worse outcome than the narrower image-only guard risks. Verified against the live BBC feed with `curl` before and after: "All done deals in July 2026" and the gossip-column item are gone from `fetch_category("transfers")`'s output; genuine single-story headlines ("Real Madrid submit €80m bid for... Rodri", "Here we go! Player X...", "...is now a done deal") are untouched. Also re-ran `fetch_category` for `football`/`sportnews`/`basketball` to confirm the universal filter doesn't starve other categories (116/89/22 items respectively, unaffected).
- **Real cause #2 — duplicate-story dedup was keyed on the wrong thing.** `agent_transfer_post.py`'s dedup only compared the LLM's freshly-generated blog title against previous *generated* titles (`title.lower()[:40]`). Since the same still-fresh BBC headline ("Ipswich Town transfer rumours: Henry Lawrence") kept getting re-surfaced by `fetch_category` across consecutive 5-10 minute cron cycles, and the article-writing LLM invents a different catchy roundup title every time, this check never fired — live evidence: **five separately-titled "breaking news" posts about the exact same Henry Lawrence/Ipswich rumour within about an hour** (`t.me/sifufinds/823,825,826,828,829`), each with its own distinct branded image and, worse, contradictory invented details (Lawrence's age stated as 19, 20, 21, and 22 across the five — a `FACT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` "never invent" violation the fact-checker gate apparently didn't catch either, since these were the LLM freely varying an unstated detail across independent runs rather than one run inventing something absent from its source text). Five different images for what's obviously one story is exactly what reads as "random/wrong images" even when each individual image is locally correct.
- **The actual fix**: added `_source_keys(post)` to `agent_transfer_post.py`, extracting normalized keys from `post["_source_items"]` (the real BBC/ESPN/etc. headline titles the article was built from, already captured by the same-day `find_source_image()` fix before being stripped). `run()` now checks these against the `posted_keys` registry in `transfer_raw_fallback_state.json` — previously only written to by the AI-outage raw-fallback path — and skips the whole cycle if the *underlying source headline* was already covered, regardless of what novel title the LLM invented this time. Records `source_keys` into the same registry after a successful publish, so raw-fallback and the normal LLM flow now share one "already covered" history (raw fallback's own `pick_fresh_raw_headline()` already read `posted_keys` — it now benefits automatically without any change on its side). Kept the file's `transfer_raw_fallback_state.json` name rather than renaming/migrating, since that would discard existing history for no functional gain.
- **Verified with a real unit test, not just by reading the diff**: replayed the exact live duplicate scenario (three posts, same source headline, three different generated titles) through `_source_keys()` + the registry-matching logic in isolation — confirmed post 1 publishes and records its key, posts 2 and 3 are correctly skipped as duplicates.
- **Both fixes needed the site's own Telegram channel as ground truth, not just `blog/posts.json`.** `posts.json` only stores the blog-facing `feature_image` (the branded graphic, always "correct" relative to its own post) — the actual Telegram/Facebook `photo_url` used per bulletin is never persisted anywhere, so the only way to see what a viewer actually saw was to pull the public channel preview (`https://t.me/s/sifufinds`, no bot token/login needed for a public channel) and cross-reference message timestamps against `datetime="..."` attributes and the git commit history for when each fix actually landed in the running GitHub Actions job (the self-perpetuating transfer-news-bot workflow only picks up new code on a fresh checkout when GitHub's cron finally cancels/restarts it — mid-loop code changes don't apply until that happens, so "the fix is committed" and "the fix is live in the currently-running job" can be hours apart on this specific workflow; see the workflow's own `cancel-in-progress` comment).
- **Auto-deploy pipeline interference mid-session, a third time**: both `news_fetcher.py` and `agent_transfer_post.py` edits got swept into two different automated `deploy: 2026-07-30 12:1x/12:2x UTC` commits (bundled alongside an unrelated new `agent_translate.py` from another in-flight session) before this session ran its own commit — same phenomenon documented three times now in this file (2026-07-24, twice on 2026-07-30 earlier). By the time this session tried to push, local history had also diverged from `origin/main` (two different unrelated commits landed there in the interim: a `leagues:` update and a `🔄 Transfer news:` cycle). Resolved with the same pattern as before: `git stash push -u` any stray uncommitted files from other concurrent processes first (never touch or discard them), `git fetch` + `git pull origin main --no-rebase` (clean merge, no real conflicts), verify both fixes' symbols still present post-merge via `grep`, restore the stash, then commit only the files this session actually authored (the knowledge-base entry) before pushing.

### Code Patterns (2026-07-30) — Phase 1 of the AI agent team expansion (news→bonus/comparison linking, Compliance Reviewer, Fact Checker, general Country Localisation agent, Featured Listings)

- User asked to build out the site's agent team against a target org chart (Strategy/SEO&GEO/Content/Product&UX/Data&Monitoring/Technical roles + Featured Listings monetization). Mapped the ~18 roles against the existing 30+ agent scripts first — several were already substantially covered (Technical SEO ≈ `seo_doctor.py`, Commercial Partnerships (outreach) ≈ `agent_brand_partnerships.py`, Broken Link/QA ≈ `validate_site.py`+`site_doctor.py`) — before building anything net-new, avoiding duplicate infrastructure.
- `gen_blog_post_pages.py`: added `BONUS_LINKS`/`COMPARISON_LINKS` tables + `_pick_news_context_links()`, extending `build_resources_box()` so `transfers`/`sportnews`-category posts (261 of them, 100% already carry a bookmaker review link via existing `bookmaker_featured`) also get a deterministic bonus-page + comparison-article link — closing the literal gap named by the user ("every news article should link to relevant reviews, comparison pages or bonus pages"). Links are 100% from static, hand-verified tables (comparison links checked against real `blog/posts.json` slugs first), never LLM-generated, per the existing `sanitize_internal_links()` precedent for why that matters here.
- New `scripts/compliance_check.py` formalizes CLAUDE.md's manual pre-publish compliance checklist (18+/BeGambleAware/no-overstated-language/`rel="sponsored"`) into a permanent guard, following `seo_check.py`/`check_indexability.py`'s exact `issue()`/CRITICAL/WARN/`--fix` shape so it slots into the same pre-deploy gate in `deploy_hostinger.yml`.
- New `agents/python/agent_fact_checker.py` — a second-pass LLM call cross-checking a drafted post's body against its own source news snippets (`post["_source_items"]`, now also carrying `description` for this purpose) before publish. Wired into `agent_sports_blog.generate_post()` itself (not into `run()`) as a single choke point, so both `agent_sports_blog.run()` and `agent_transfer_post.py` (which imports `generate_post` directly) are covered without duplicating the gate. On FLAG, `generate_post()` returns `None` — reusing the existing "nothing publishable this run" signal already handled correctly everywhere `post is None` is checked, rather than inventing a new failure mode.
- New `agents/python/agent_translate.py` — started as a European-Portuguese-only agent per the initial plan, generalized mid-session to a `--locale`-parameterized Country Localisation agent after the user said so directly. Good call: discovered `fr`/`de` (the only locales with any translated content) have only 168 translated entries against 813 total posts — they have a bigger untranslated backlog than the supposedly-empty `es`/`pt`/`sw` locales combined, so a single-locale agent would have been solving the wrong problem. `pick_locale()` auto-selects whichever locale has the biggest backlog when `--locale` is omitted.
- Featured Listings: `data/featured_listings.json` (schema: slot/brand_slug/sponsored/starts_at/ends_at/criteria_note) is the single source of truth, managed via new `agent_brand_partnerships.py --manage-listings/--add-listing/--remove-listing` flags (enforces one-brand-per-slot, requires `criteria_note` for transparency, validates ISO 8601 `ends_at`). Rendering deliberately scoped to ONE safe surface this pass: `bookmaker_page_template.py`'s `render_bookmaker_page()` (a pure function, already used by `agent_brand_discovery.py`, safe to test in isolation) reads the active listing for a brand and renders a "⭐ Featured Partner · Sponsored" badge next to the existing "🆕 New Listing" verdict badge. Did NOT attempt sitewide rendering (homepage hero, listing tables on `index.html`/`countries/*/index.html`) — see Errors to Never Repeat below for why that was the right call, not a shortcut.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-30, same session) — compliance_check.py's first draft had two real false-positive bugs, both caught by actually running it against the live site before shipping

- **Banned-phrase list flagged legitimate copy.** `"guaranteed profit"` seemed like an obvious red flag for gambling-outcome overstatement, but a live run immediately flagged two accurate, harmless uses: `guides/accumulator-tips/index.html` describing Cash Out ("cash out for a guaranteed profit before the final leg settles" — a real, correct description of a real bookmaker feature) and `tools/odds-calculator/index.html` explaining bookmaker margin ("the excess is their guaranteed profit edge"). Dropped that specific phrase from the banned list entirely rather than trying to special-case around it — the lesson is to run any new banned-phrase/regex compliance check against the full live site before trusting it, not just unit-test it against synthetic bad examples.
