- **`.htaccess` slug extraction wasn't scoped to the right section.** `_masked_brand_slugs()` initially regex-matched every `RewriteRule ^<slug>/?$` in the whole `.htaccess` file, not just the "AFFILIATE LINK MASKING" block — it also picked up unrelated internal short-URL redirects with the identical shape (`^cricket/?$`, `^f1/?$`, `^afcon/?$`, category-page shortcuts), producing false "missing rel=sponsored" warnings on ordinary internal navigation links. Fixed by scoping the regex to only the text between the "AFFILIATE LINK MASKING" header and the next `# ──` section header. General lesson: when deriving a check's input list from a shared config file by pattern-matching a line shape, always scope to the specific section that shape is documented to mean — the same shape can recur elsewhere for an unrelated purpose.
- **Bare `python3` on this machine is 3.9 and cannot run this repo's agent scripts** (confirms/reinforces the 2026-07-24 entry) — `agent_fact_checker.py`/`agent_translate.py` both use `dict | None` union syntax (3.10+), and `utils/logger.py` already does too, so even an unrelated new script crashes on import under the system Python. Always use `.venv/bin/python3` (3.13 in this repo) for any local test/run of `agents/python/*.py`, not the bare `python3` on PATH.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-30, same session) — this repo's working directory is actively shared with concurrent automation, confirmed live mid-session

- Multiple times during this session, a newly-created or freshly-edited file (`agents/python/agent_translate.py`, then separately `agents/python/agent_brand_partnerships.py`, `agents/python/utils/bookmaker_page_template.py`, `data/featured_listings.json`) either got swept into a generic `deploy: <timestamp> UTC` bot commit before this session's own `git add`/`commit` ran, or transiently showed as reverted-to-HEAD in one tool call and then correctly-edited again in the very next one — a real race with concurrent automation/another session writing to the same checkout, not a tool malfunction. One case (`agent_brand_partnerships.py`) briefly failed a live CLI test with "unrecognized arguments" for flags that were, moments later, confirmed present and working — caused by reading the file mid-overwrite.
- **Practical handling that worked**: never assume a `git add`/`git commit` sequence landed as intended — always verify with `git log --oneline -3 -- <path>` and/or `git show HEAD:<path> | grep <symbol>` afterward. If a file that should show as modified instead shows clean, check `git log` before concluding something went wrong — it's usually already safely committed by the concurrent bot, not lost. `git pull origin main --no-rebase -q` before every commit attempt kept merges clean throughout (no real conflicts hit this session, only fast-forwards).

### Market Intelligence (2026-07-30, same session) — brand/review data is duplicated across 3 separate hardcoded Python lists, a real blocker for Phase 2 content writers

- `gen_bk_reviews.py` (legacy, 3 brands), `agent_brand_reviews.py` (809 lines, its own `BRANDS` list, different schema again), and `agent_telegram_offers.py` (promo-copy `BRANDS` list, third schema) each independently hardcode overlapping facts about the same bookmakers. `data/bookmaker_links.json` is the only genuine source of truth, but it only tracks link/status metadata, not review copy/bonus terms. Before building the Phase 2 Casino Review Writer, Bonus Content Writer, or Betting Guide Writer, this needs consolidating into one canonical `data/bookmakers.json` — otherwise a 4th writer just adds a 4th list to reconcile. Documented as an explicit blocker in CLAUDE.md's new roadmap section rather than worked around.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-30, later same day) — the Phase 1 agent-team roadmap table itself had two factual errors, found only by re-grepping the actual codebase instead of trusting the earlier session's summary

- User asked to fix any agents missing from the roadmap "lists" and finish any incomplete items. A fresh audit of every file in `agents/python/*.py` and `scripts/*.py` against the org chart (not just re-reading what Phase 1 claimed) found the roadmap table was itself wrong in two places: `scripts/geo_check.py` already substantially covers the "GEO Optimisation Agent" role (AI-crawler robots.txt access, llms.txt validity, run via `seo_geo_biweekly.yml`) but had been listed as "Phase 2, doesn't exist yet"; `agent_brand_scraper.py` already does daily confidence-gated bonus/offer verification but "Affiliate Offer Monitoring Agent" had been listed as "genuinely missing." Also found `agent5_plan.py` ("Growth Planner") fully implements the CMO Agent role in code but was never wired into any GitHub Actions workflow — dead code, not a running agent, despite existing since before this session.
- **Lesson**: when auditing "what already exists" against a target list of roles, actually `grep`/read every file in the relevant directories and check what each one's docstring says it does, rather than relying on a summary written earlier in the same or a prior session — a summary re-states its own prior blind spots as fact if not re-verified against the source.

### Code Patterns (2026-07-30, later same day) — Phase 2: CMO Agent scheduled, plus 3 new agents (Keyword Research, SERP Monitoring, Performance & CWV) for the roles confirmed genuinely missing

- `.github/workflows/agent5_plan.yml` — straightforward fix, no code changes needed: `agent5_plan.py` already worked when run manually (verified live: produced a real weekly plan with 5 keyword opportunities and 3 growth actions from one `ask()` call), it just needed a cron (`0 5 * * 1`, avoiding collision with `brand_discovery.yml`'s Monday 03:30 UTC slot) and safety-net registration.
- `agents/python/agent_keyword_research.py` + `agent_serp_monitor.py` both build on two new small public helpers added to `utils/serp_research.py`: `find_site_position(results, domain)` (1-indexed rank of a domain within a free-search result list, or `None`) and `paa_hints_from_results(results)` (exposes the existing private `_paa_from_snippets()` logic to callers that only have a result list, not the full `research()` block). Verified `find_site_position`'s regression-detection math in isolation (3→9 delta-6 flags, 3→unranked flags, 3→5 delta-2 doesn't) before trusting it against live data, since a false-negative here would mean regressions silently go unflagged.
- Live-tested `agent_keyword_research.py` for real: found SifuFinds does not currently rank in the top 10 for "best betting sites nigeria 2026" or "best betting bonus nigeria 2026" — genuinely useful competitive intelligence (competitor URLs: goal.com, mybettingsites.com, champsbase.com, tips.gg), not just a working-code demo.
- `scripts/performance_check.py` uses Google's free PageSpeed Insights API v5 (no key required, `PAGESPEED_API_KEY` env var optional for a higher rate limit) rather than adding a Node/Lighthouse CLI dependency to this Python-only pipeline. Deliberately built as a monitoring check (own schedule, `performance_check.yml`), not a pre-deploy gate — probing the live site mid-deploy would measure a race against the exact content being pushed, not a real signal (see the script's own docstring). Hit a live `429 Too Many Requests` from Google's unauthenticated tier during testing (expected/handled — logs a WARN, exits 0, tries again next scheduled run); verified the metric-extraction and CRITICAL/WARN threshold logic separately against a synthetic Lighthouse response shape since the live call couldn't complete.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-07-31) — the site-wide "reliable paid Claude fallback" tier had never actually run in any CI workflow, and generic-social-image fallback was silently posting a 404

- Following up on the transfer-news image fixes above, verified them against ~18 hours of real production Telegram history (`t.me/s/sifufinds`) rather than a single test post. All three held up: no more recap/gossip items polluting captions, no more duplicate-story bursts, every post carried an image. But that same live check surfaced two more real, independent bugs while confirming the ones already fixed.
- **Bug: `GENERIC_SOCIAL_IMAGE`/Instagram's fallback image pointed at a file that has never existed.** `https://sifufinds.com/assets/social-card.jpg` 404s (confirmed via `curl -sI`) — `assets/social-card.jpg` doesn't exist anywhere in the repo. Hardcoded in three places: `agent_transfer_post.py`'s `GENERIC_SOCIAL_IMAGE`, and the Instagram fallback in both `agent3_social.py` and `agent3_social_telethon.py`. Caught live: a raw-fallback transfer post (`t.me/sifufinds/841`) had no source photo, correctly fell back to the generic card, Telegram couldn't fetch the 404'd URL, and `send_photo_to_channel()`'s own documented graceful-degradation ("falls back further to a text-only message if the photo send fails") silently dropped the image (post itself wasn't lost). **Fix**: repointed all three to `assets/og-image.png` — the site's real default social image, already used correctly in `index.html`'s own `og:image` meta tag, confirmed live 200. Verified live: every bot post since the fix (14:06 UTC onward, checked through the following morning) carries an image; zero text-only degradations.
- **Bigger bug, found while asking "why has this site posted zero new full articles — any category, not just transfers — in 18+ hours?"**: `agents/python/llm.py`'s fallback chain is documented as Groq (4 free models) → Claude (paid, described as the "reliable" tier) → Gemini (2 free models). `agents/python/requirements.txt` — the single shared requirements file nearly every content-generation workflow in this repo installs from (`pip install -r agents/python/requirements.txt`, confirmed via grep across `.github/workflows/*.yml`) — **has never listed the `anthropic` package**. `llm.py` wraps the import in `try: import anthropic ... except ImportError: pass`, so on every real CI runner `_anthropic_client` silently stayed `None` forever, with zero log output pointing at why — the module just quietly had one less fallback tier than its own docstring promised. Separately confirmed via `gh secret list` that no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` secret exists in this repo at all, so even after fixing the package gap, this tier is still inert until a real key is added — that part needs the user's own action (a real API credential), not something fixable by an agent. With the "reliable" tier dead both ways, the whole site has been running on Groq's + Gemini's free-tier quotas alone, shared across every single scheduled workflow (transfers, football, sportnews, betting, tennis, cricket, rugby, boxing, f1, igaming, translations, fact-checking, SERP research, etc.) — more than enough concurrent load to explain a genuine multi-hour exhaustion window without anything being individually broken.
- **The actual fix (the part fixable without a new credential)**: added `anthropic>=0.40.0` to `agents/python/requirements.txt` (necessary groundwork — inert without the key, but required before the key can ever help). Replaced the silent `except ImportError: pass` in `llm.py` with a loud `print()` warning covering both failure shapes (key set but package missing / key not set at all) — this exact bug's defining trait was that it was invisible in every single workflow run's logs for however long it's been broken; a missing tier must now announce itself on every run instead of only being discoverable by hours of manual forensic digging through Telegram history and cross-referencing `posts.json` timestamps.
- **Verified, not just read**: confirmed the warning fires when the package is genuinely absent (via `/opt/homebrew/bin/python3.11`, which has no `anthropic` installed — matches actual CI's fresh-`pip install` state before this fix) and that the client initializes cleanly with no warning when the package is present and a key is set (via the system's default `python3`, which does have `anthropic` 0.76.0 installed locally) — neither single local interpreter had both a modern-enough Python *and* the package, so both halves of the fix needed separate verification against different interpreters to actually prove out.
- **Left open, deliberately not worked around**: the missing `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` GitHub secret. An agent should never fabricate or guess at a real API credential — this needs the account owner to generate a real key at console.anthropic.com and add it via `gh secret set ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (typed directly into their own terminal, never pasted into a chat transcript) or the GitHub UI. Until that happens, the package fix is necessary-but-not-sufficient — Claude fallback stays inert and the site remains fully dependent on Groq/Gemini free tiers.
- **Auto-deploy pipeline interference, a fourth time**: both fixes (`requirements.txt`, `llm.py`) were swept into another automated `deploy: 2026-07-31 07:20 UTC` commit before this session's own commit landed — by now a fully expected, routine part of working in this repo (see the three prior same-day entries above). Handled the same way each time: verify the swept-in content is complete and correct via `grep`/`git show` before trusting it, don't re-commit something that's already committed, just confirm and move on.

### Code Patterns (2026-07-31, later same day) — Geo homepage routing + rotating 3-brand offer popup, both wired at the shared-infra level instead of per-page

- User asked for two behaviours: (1) every visitor's own detected country becomes their homepage (e.g. a Kenyan visitor lands on `/countries/kenya/`, not the generic `index.html`), for all 23 supported countries including Nigeria; (2) after 30 s on any landing page, show a popup of 3 bookmaker offers, rotating so the 3 shown keep changing. Both are documented as new STANDING RULEs in CLAUDE.md ("Geo Homepage Routing" and "Rotating 3-Brand Offer Popup").
- **Reused existing geo-IP infra instead of building new.** `assets/shared.js` already had `waitForCountry()`/`_geoFetch` (ipapi.co, 2 s timeout, `localStorage['sf_cty']`, 23-country `_SUPPORTED_CTYS` set) wired up for patching bookmaker data in place — it just never redirected anywhere. The new redirect lives as an inline `<script>` at the very top of `index.html`'s `<head>` (before `shared.js` loads) and deliberately writes to the *same* `sf_cty` localStorage key, so when `shared.js` parses later in `<body>`, its own `_geoFetch` IIFE sees the key already set and skips a redundant second network call — single source of truth, no duplicate geo-IP lookups per pageview.
- **Decided against a special-cased "generic homepage" fallback path.** Considered keeping non-African/undetected visitors on the plain `index.html` instead of redirecting them to Nigeria's page too, but that would only be distinguishable on a *first* visit — after that, `localStorage['sf_cty']` already holds `'NG'` for both "genuinely Nigerian" and "detection failed/unsupported country," identically, since that's how the pre-existing `waitForCountry()` contract already worked. Two behaviourally different code paths that become indistinguishable after visit #1 is the kind of inconsistency worth avoiding — went with one uniform rule instead (resolved country, defaulting to NG exactly like the rest of the site already does, always redirects) so first-visit and return-visit behaviour is identical. Escape hatches (`?cty=XX`, `?intl=1`) exist for anyone who deliberately wants the generic pan-African page.
- **Found and fixed a real pre-existing gap while wiring the popup up: the 23 `countries/<slug>/index.html` pages (and their generator, `generate_country_pages.py`) never had `?v=` cache-busting on `assets/shared.js`/`assets/shared.css` at all** — every other page on the site (~1,449 of them) already followed the `shared.js?v=N` convention, but country pages used a bare, unversioned `<script src="../../assets/shared.js">`. This only mattered because these pages are now the actual homepage for most real visitors after the routing change above — without a version bump, a browser with a cached copy might not pick up the new popup code promptly. Fixed both the generator and all 23 already-generated pages in the same pass, then bumped the version everywhere (`shared.js?v=10→11`, `shared.css?v=8→9`) across the whole site (~1,470 files + 6 more Python generators that also hardcode the tag) via `sed`, not by hand-patching a subset.
- **Popup reuses the exact existing card template** (`.fc`/`.fc-img`/`.fc-body`/`.fc-off`/`.gbtn`, the same markup `renderFeatCards()` already renders on the homepage) rather than inventing new markup — same `rel="noopener noreferrer sponsored"` + direct `b.url` href convention every other bookmaker card on the site uses. Rotation is a Fisher-Yates shuffle of the visitor's country `BOOKS[cty]` list, first 3 taken; correctly degrades to *not showing* the popup at all (rather than showing fewer than 3) for the 2 countries (Ethiopia, DR Congo) that currently only have 2 bookmakers listed. Shown once per browser tab via `sessionStorage`, not once per pageview, to avoid being spammy across normal in-session navigation.
- Ran the full pre-deploy gate (`validate_site.py`, `seo_check.py`, `compliance_check.py`, `check_indexability.py`) after both changes — all passed (0 critical everywhere; the pre-existing high/medium `seo_check.py` findings are unrelated duplicate-blog-title issues, not caused by this change). No JS runtime (`node`) is available in this sandboxed environment to execute the code directly, so correctness was verified by careful manual review of the diff plus the static validators rather than a live browser run — flag this to the user/next session if a real browser smoke test is wanted before the next deploy.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-01) — a fix landing in one code path but not its sibling, twice more in the same session

- **Duplicate-content dedup fix from 2026-07-30 never reached the general blog agent.** `agent_transfer_post.py` got real source-headline dedup (`_source_keys()`/`posted_keys`) after five differently-titled posts covered the same Henry Lawrence/Ipswich rumour in an hour. But `agent_sports_blog.py`'s own `run()` — what `breaking_news.yml` actually runs 3x/day — still only compared the LLM's *own invented* title's first 40 chars against recent posts, which structurally cannot catch "same real story, fresh LLM phrasing." A routine site-wide scan (`scripts/seo_check.py`) found 56 duplicate-title post pairs as the direct, predictable result (e.g. 5+ near-identical "Bruno Guimaraes to Arsenal" articles). **Fix:** extracted the dedup logic into `agents/python/utils/story_dedup.py`, shared by both agents against one registry (`agents/python/covered_stories_state.json`, migrated from the old `transfer_raw_fallback_state.json`, 180 keys preserved). **Lesson: when a duplicate-content/dedup bug gets fixed in one content-generating agent, grep for every other agent that also calls an LLM to write "fresh" content and check whether the same gap exists there too — don't assume a fix in the specific bot that happened to trigger the bug report covers every bot capable of triggering the same bug.**
- **Same pattern again, smaller: the FAQ "Q:"/"A:" label leak was fixed in the JSON-LD schema extractor but never in the actual page renderer.** `extract_faq_schema()` in `gen_blog_post_pages.py` already stripped literal `Q:`/`A:` prefixes from FAQ text on 2026-07-26 (its own comment says so: "leaks into both the visible page and acceptedAnswer.text"), but `markdown_to_html()` — the function that actually produces what readers see — was never touched. A GEO scan on 2026-08-01 found 3 live posts showing "Q: What is the transfer window?" verbatim to readers. Fixed by applying the identical regex (`^Q[:.]\s*` / `^A[:.]\s*`) to the `<h3>` and `<p>` branches of `markdown_to_html()`. **Lesson: when a comment says "this also leaks into the visible page," go check the visible-page renderer too — don't assume the schema fix and the display fix are the same commit just because they were diagnosed together.**
- **The daily automated SEO auto-heal (`scripts/seo_check.py --fix`, run daily via `daily_seo_doctor.yml`) was itself silently drifting `blog/posts-data.js` out of sync with `blog/posts.json` on every single fix it applied**, because it only wrote `posts.json` and never regenerated the file://-fallback copy the way `agent_sports_blog.save_posts()` does. A self-healing mechanism that itself introduces drift every time it heals something is worse than doing nothing — found while double-checking the 12 title/excerpt fixes this session applied. Fixed by writing both files in the same `--fix` block.
- **X (Twitter) posting had failed on every single scheduled run for 2+ days straight** (`aria-disabled='true'` on the Post button both before AND after the click, confirmed across 30 consecutive run logs going back to 2026-07-30) before this was caught — because GitHub Actions reports this as a normal red ✗, not a special alarm, and nothing was cross-referencing "how many consecutive runs has this workflow failed" against a threshold. Root cause: the code only waited a fixed ~1.5s/4s after typing/clicking before checking whether the post actually went through, nowhere near long enough for X's own client-side validation (character count incl. t.co-shortened URLs, link-preview card attachment) to finish settling the button's disabled state. Fixed by polling the button's `aria-disabled` attribute (up to ~10s) instead of guessing a fixed wait, and by verifying the compose box's `innerText` actually contains the typed text before attempting submission at all (with one retry) — the earlier fixed-wait approach could silently submit against a stale/empty editor with zero exception raised. **Lesson: "did the last N runs of workflow X all fail" is a distinct, valuable question from "did the most recent run fail" — a periodic full-history sweep across every scheduled workflow (not just the newest run) is what actually catches a multi-day silent content-channel outage like this one.**

### Code Patterns (2026-08-01) — local, on-demand open-weight LLM fallback with zero cost to the common path

- The site runs ~10 different scheduled agents off one shared free `GROQ_API_KEY`, with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` never configured as a GitHub secret at all and Gemini's free tier gated behind billing that isn't enabled — so once Groq's daily quota is exhausted (very plausible on a busy day across that many agents), Breaking News Bot and Transfer News Bot both went fully dark (33+ hours with zero new Transfer News content, confirmed via `git log --grep` and `transfer_raw_fallback_state.json`'s mtime) with no paid/free alternative to fall through to. User explicitly declined both paid options (an Anthropic key, enabling Gemini billing) and declined a second free-but-still-requires-signup option (OpenRouter/Cerebras), choosing a fully self-hosted open-weight model in CI instead — no account, no key, genuinely $0.
- **Key design decision: install/start/pull Ollama lazily inside `llm.py`'s `_ensure_ollama()`, called only from within `_ask_with_fallback()` after every Groq model has already failed — never as a workflow-level YAML setup step.** This means zero workflow files needed to change, and the common case (a Groq model succeeds, which is most of the time) pays no extra latency or CI minutes at all. The alternative (a `.github/actions/setup-ollama` composite action added to every content-generating workflow) would have added ~1-2 min of install+pull overhead to *every single run* of ~8 workflows regardless of whether the fallback was ever needed — much worse trade-off for a tier that should rarely trigger.
- Model choice: `llama3.2:3b` (~2GB, Ollama's default quantization) — small enough to download and run CPU-only inference within a GitHub-hosted runner's time/disk budget, at a real quality cost versus the 70B-class Groq models. This tier is a last resort that produces *something* instead of nothing, not a quality-equivalent replacement.
- `_ollama_setup_attempted` module-level flag prevents retrying a failed install/pull a second time within the same process (e.g. no internet egress, disk full) — once it's confirmed not to work in this environment, every subsequent call in the same run falls through past it immediately to Claude/Gemini instead of re-attempting a multi-minute install that already failed.
- **This machine's local automated `deploy: <timestamp> UTC` pipeline (documented in CLAUDE.md) swept up this exact change mid-session, before it could be committed with a descriptive message** — same "auto-deploy pipeline interference" pattern documented multiple times in the 2026-07-31 entries above, confirmed again by checking `git show <sha> --stat` to verify the swept-in diff was complete and correct rather than assuming. Verified via `git log --oneline --all -- agents/python/llm.py` and `git show HEAD:agents/python/llm.py | grep` rather than trusting `git status`/`git diff HEAD` alone (both reported "clean" once the auto-pipeline had already committed and pushed, which is expected but easy to misread as "my change never happened" if you don't check *what* is at HEAD).
- Not live-tested end-to-end in this session by explicit user choice (declined installing Ollama on their local Mac for a test, correctly noting the real deployment target is ephemeral GitHub Actions runners, not their laptop) — verified via careful manual code review only (response parsing matches Ollama's documented `/api/chat` non-streaming schema, `num_predict` is the correct option name for max tokens, exception handling falls through to Claude/Gemini on any failure). Flag to the next session: this tier's first real-world exercise will be the next time Groq's quota is genuinely exhausted on a scheduled run — worth checking that specific run's logs for `[llm] Installing local Ollama fallback` / `[llm] Pulling local fallback model` the first time it fires, since install-script and model-pull behavior in the *actual* GitHub Actions environment (vs. reasoning about it) is the one part of this fix that couldn't be verified without either a live CI run or installing on a real machine.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-01, later same day) — a "23 countries" claim that was quietly only 6

- **`agent_keyword_research.py` and `agent5_plan.py` both imported `config.COUNTRIES` for their country loop, but that list only has 6 markets (Nigeria, Kenya, South Africa, Ghana, Tanzania, Uganda) with hardcoded currency/bookmaker/payment fields — not the 23 the site actually serves** (the real list lives in `index.html`'s geo-redirect `MAP`/`CTY_NAMES` objects and `gen_blog_post_pages.py`'s `COUNTRY_LINKS`, per the 2026-07-31 Geo Homepage Routing rule). This meant the keyword-research backlog silently never touched 17 of the 23 countries the site has dedicated pages for — found when asked to build a keyword agent covering "all 23 plus African countries" and checking what the existing agent actually looped over. `agent1_content.py` (`random.choice(COUNTRIES)`) has the same latent gap and was left alone this session (its country entries carry currency/bookmaker/payment fields the 17 missing countries don't have populated — expanding `config.COUNTRIES` itself would need that data backfilled first, a separate task). **Fix applied:** new `agents/python/utils/countries.py` — a plain `{code, name, slug}` list mirroring `index.html`'s `MAP` exactly, no per-country content-generation fields, safe for any agent that only needs "which countries does the site serve" rather than "what currency/bookmakers does this market have." `agent_keyword_research.py` now imports from there instead of `config.COUNTRIES`. **Lesson: before trusting a list named `COUNTRIES` (or any similarly generic collection) as "all the countries the site supports," check what it's actually used for elsewhere — a list built for one agent's specific data needs (currency/bookmaker/payment) is not automatically the canonical set of countries the site serves everywhere else.**
- **`utils/news_fetcher.py`'s "Punch Sports (Nigeria)" RSS feed (`https://punchng.com/topics/sports/feed/`, tagged `category="football"` in `FEEDS`) intermittently serves plain Nigerian political/legal headlines, not sports** — confirmed live 2026-08-01: a `fetch_category("football")` call returned "Criminals have no tribe, religion, says Shettima", "Presidency rebukes Catholic bishops after Onaiyekan's outburst", "More heat on Atiku as APC backs Obasanjo", and "Historic rise of Nigeria's most powerful women in law" mixed in with genuine WAFCON/Premier League stories, all tagged category="football" since that's what the feed's category label promises. Found while building `agent_trending_keywords.py`, which turns fresh headline titles directly into keyword topics — without a relevance check, political headlines would have become nonsensical betting keywords (and worse, this same feed already feeds `agent_sports_blog.py`'s actual football posts, so the same pollution risk exists there, just less visible since the LLM's "only reference real named stories" instruction likely self-corrects rather than writing about Nigerian politics under a football byline — not verified either way this session). **Mitigation applied (scoped to the new agent only, not a `news_fetcher.py` fix):** `agent_trending_keywords.py._looks_sports_related()` requires a sports/competition/betting vocabulary match before any headline becomes a keyword topic, rather than trusting the feed's own category tag. **Flag for a future session: consider whether `agent_sports_blog.py` needs the same relevance guard before calling `fetch_category()`, or whether the Punch feed itself should be swapped/removed from `FEEDS`** — this session didn't touch that broader surface since it wasn't the reported bug, but the underlying feed-tagging trust issue is the same one that bit Reuters/Football365/Goal.com's RSS feeds going stale/wrong before (2026-07-24 entry) — feed category labels need periodic spot-checking against actual content, not one-time trust.

### Code Patterns (2026-08-01, later same day) — trending (news-driven) keyword agent, distinct from the evergreen backlog agent

- Built `agents/python/agent_trending_keywords.py` as a sibling to `agent_keyword_research.py`, not a merge into it — same free-first SERP-check pattern (`fc_search`/`find_site_position` from `utils/serp_research.py`, no LLM call, $0 cost) but a genuinely different data source: it re-seeds its keyword candidates from `utils/news_fetcher.fetch_category()`'s fresh headlines every run (football/betting/worldcup2026/basketball categories) instead of a fixed template list, so the keywords stay current with whatever's actually happening (a live fixture, a transfer, a tournament result) rather than static "best betting sites {country} 2026" phrasing that barely changes month to month. Persists to `trending_keywords.json`, dict-keyed by `f"{country_code}:{topic_lower}"` (mirrors `keyword_opportunities.json`'s dict-of-keyword shape) so re-checking the same trending pair updates in place rather than duplicating.
- `_extract_topic()` heuristic for turning a headline into a short keyword subject: prefer a regex-matched "X vs Y" fixture (clean, betting-relevant on its own), otherwise strip known source-name suffixes (" | Goal.com", " - BBC Sport" etc.) and generic filler words (predictions/tips/today/betting/odds/prepositions — the intent-modifier words the keyword templates append separately, stripped here specifically so they don't end up doubled, e.g. avoiding "X Betting betting tips Nigeria"), capped at 8 words. Good enough for a free heuristic; not NLP-grade entity extraction, and still lets through some low-relevance-but-technically-sports items (US college basketball recruiting news, for instance) since the goal was filtering out *actively wrong* content (politics) rather than perfectly ranking relevance.
- Wired the output into `agent5_plan.py` (the Growth Planner / weekly editorial calendar): added `_top_gap_keywords()`/`_top_trending()` helpers that load `keyword_opportunities.json`/`trending_keywords.json` via the existing generic `load_json()` helper and inject the real backlog into the LLM prompt, explicitly instructing it to base `weekly_editorial_calendar`/`keyword_gap_opportunities` on that real data "rather than inventing keywords from scratch" — previously `agent5_plan.py` had a `keyword_gap_opportunities` output field in its JSON schema but nothing upstream ever fed it real keyword research, so the LLM was guessing every week. This is the actual "keywords get used for content writing" connection point, since nothing else in the pipeline currently turns `weekly_plan.json` into scheduled blog posts automatically (that remains a human/other-agent consumption step, not yet automated — flagged as a gap, not fixed this session since it's a separate, bigger pipeline-wiring task).
- Scheduled via new `.github/workflows/agent_trending_keywords.yml`, cron `0 2 * * *` UTC daily — 15/30 min offset from `agent_keyword_research.yml` (01:30) and `agent_serp_monitor.yml` (01:45) respectively, same commit-with-retry pattern as every other research agent workflow in this repo (5 attempts, merge-and-retry on push rejection, never fails the job on a rate-limited/empty run).
- Confirmed `agents/python/.github/workflows/` (a nested nested directory containing 5 stale workflow YAMLs, last touched in the original May 2026 scaffolding commit) is dead — GitHub Actions only reads workflows from the repo-root `.github/workflows/`, so anything only present under `agents/python/.github/workflows/` never actually runs. Did not touch or clean it up this session (out of scope for the keyword-research task), but future sessions should not assume a workflow file only found there is live, and CLAUDE.md's Affiliate Link Masking rule currently cites `agents/python/.github/workflows/agent3_social.yml` as "the one actually wired into" — that line is very likely stale/incorrect and worth re-verifying against the real root-level `agent3_social.yml` next time that section is touched.

### Market Intelligence (2026-08-01) — first real ranking snapshot across all 23 countries

- Ran both keyword agents live this session to seed real data rather than reporting on the pre-existing 2-country sample. Result across 28 evergreen money keywords (Nigeria/Kenya/Ghana/South Africa/Tanzania, `best betting sites/bonus {country} 2026`, `{country} football betting tips today`, `how to bet online {country} beginners guide`, `best live odds {country} 2026`, `safest betting apps {country}` templates) and 23 trending news-paired keywords (one per country): **0 of 51 checked keywords currently show SifuFinds ranking** in the free DuckDuckGo-based check `find_site_position()` uses (not literal Google SERP position — a proxy; see the existing `serp_rank_history.json` evergreen watchlist for the few keywords that DO rank there: `bet9ja review 2026` #3, and `accumulator calculator africa` had been #4 until this session's `agent_serp_monitor.py` run flagged it as a fresh regression, now unranked). Top competitors dominating the unranked evergreen keywords are consistently Goal.com, mybettingsites.com, champsbase.com, gamblingnigeria.com, and country-specific "top100bookmakers"/"ratingbet"-style aggregator sites — none of which are SifuFinds' current 65+ blog posts or bookmaker review pages targeting these exact head-term phrasings. **Actionable read: SifuFinds' content strategy so far has been news/blog-post-shaped (individual match tips, transfer stories) rather than head-term-shaped (the exact "best betting sites Nigeria 2026" style pages competitors rank with) — the gap isn't visibility, it's that dedicated pages/posts targeting these specific head terms largely don't exist yet on the site.** This is a 51-keyword sample out of the now much larger 138-evergreen + daily-refreshing-trending backlog; both agents are scheduled daily and will keep expanding real coverage, so treat this as a first data point to act on, not a final verdict.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-02) — the "button never enables" symptom was a hard limit, not a timing race; the 2026-08-01 polling fix was necessary but revealed the real bug instead of hiding it

- **The 2026-08-01 fix (poll the Post button's `aria-disabled` up to 10s instead of a blind fixed wait) did not resolve X posting** — but it proved invaluable anyway, because its new diagnostics (`text registered: True`, `button enabled after poll: False`) ruled out the original "slow link-preview card" theory conclusively: text was confirmed present, a full 10s of polling still never saw the button enable. That's not a timing race, that's a hard limit being hit.
- **Real root cause: Twitter counts most emoji/symbols as 2 characters, not 1** — verified by fetching Twitter's own published `twitter-text` v3 config (`https://raw.githubusercontent.com/twitter/twitter-text/master/config/v3.json`): `maxWeightedTweetLength=280`, `defaultWeight=200` (weight 2 on a 100-scale), and only four specific Unicode ranges (0-4351 covering Latin/Greek/Cyrillic/Hebrew/Arabic/etc., plus three small General Punctuation ranges) get the low weight of 1 — everything else, including every emoji block, is weight 2. Our `_tweet_len()` used plain `len()` (weight 1 for everything except URLs), so a flag-emoji-heavy tip template (🇳🇬🇰🇪🇿🇦🇬🇭) read as 279/280 by our count while X's real weighted count was 282 — genuinely over the limit, button correctly and permanently disabled by X's own client-side validation, with zero error surfaced anywhere in the failure path.
- **The exact same naive `_tweet_len()`/`_trim_to_limit()` pair was independently copy-pasted into three files** (`agent_twitter_posts.py`, `agent_match_post.py`, `agent_transfer_post.py`) — the third instance in two days of "a fix/bug exists in one code path but not its structurally-identical sibling" in this codebase (see the two 2026-08-01 entries above: duplicate-content dedup, FAQ Q:/A: labels). Extracted into `agents/python/utils/tweet_text.py` this time specifically to make a fourth recurrence structurally impossible — every social-posting agent that adds tweet-length logic in the future should import this, not write its own. **Lesson: this codebase's own established pattern (per its "SEO Health Is Continuously Self-Healing" table) is right — when you fix one thing, grep for every sibling file with the same shape of logic before calling it done, every time, no exceptions.**
- **Live-validated the Ollama fallback tier from the same session's earlier fix** (2026-08-01 llm.py change) for the first time in real production: a Breaking News Bot run at 20:16 UTC exhausted all 4 Groq models, logged `[llm] ... trying local Ollama fallback` → `Installing local Ollama fallback` → `Starting local Ollama server` → `Pulling local fallback model llama3.2:3b`, and ~4 minutes later successfully produced real article content that reached the fact-checker (which then correctly held those specific drafts back for unverifiable numeric claims — the quality gate working as designed, not a fallback failure). Confirms the whole on-demand install→pull→serve→infer chain works end-to-end in the actual GitHub Actions environment, the one part of that fix that couldn't be verified without a live run.

### Code Patterns (2026-08-02) — noindex + canonical_override is the safe way to clean up duplicate content after the fact

- 56 duplicate-title post pairs (14 connected clusters via union-find on the pairwise overlaps `seo_check.py` reports) predated the 2026-08-01 dedup fix and needed cleanup — one cluster alone had 17 posts covering the same Celtic/Kasper Hogh transfer saga under differently-LLM-invented titles. **Decided against deletion (breaks existing links/traffic, no upside) and against .htaccess redirects (bigger, harder-to-reverse action for a same-site duplicate-content problem, not a moved/removed page).** Used a mechanism this codebase already had fully built but never exercised against real duplicates: `posts.json`'s `noindex`/`canonical_override` fields, already read correctly by `gen_blog_post_pages.py` and already validated by `seo_check.py`'s `noindex_without_canonical`/`noindex_not_in_html` CRITICAL checks (a good sign an intended mechanism existed for exactly this — it just needed to be applied). Primary-per-cluster picked as the oldest `published_at` (most likely to already hold any real search ranking); everything else in the cluster gets `noindex: true` + `canonical_override` pointing at the primary, stays fully live for existing direct links. Cleared all 56 MEDIUM findings down to 0.
- Reusable pattern for the next time this recurs (it will, since new posts keep publishing): `seo_check.py --report` → union-find the pairwise `potential_duplicate` overlaps into clusters → oldest-wins primary → bulk-set `noindex`/`canonical_override` on the rest → regenerate → re-validate. Scripted as a one-off in this session, not (yet) wired into `seo_check.py --fix` itself — worth promoting into the script directly if this keeps needing to be re-run manually.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-02, later same session) — a same-session fix broke deploy for ~1hr; the "auto-retry everything, no exceptions" standing rule had two real gaps

- **The noindex+canonical_override cleanup (above) broke deploy for about an hour.** Setting `noindex: true` unconditionally on a post cascades to every locale variant `gen_blog_post_pages.py` renders for it, not just English — `canonical_override` was already correctly guarded to `locale == 'en'` only, but `noindex` wasn't. One of the 35 posts (`bet9ja-vs-sportybet-nigeria-2026-comparison`) has fr/de translations; its noindex target (`bet9ja-vs-sportybet-nigeria-2026`) has none. The cascade would have deindexed the only fr/de coverage of that topic with zero replacement — `check_indexability.py`'s pre-deploy gate correctly caught this as CRITICAL `unexpected_noindex` and blocked every deploy from 23:40 UTC until the fix landed. **Caught by proactively re-running the full workflow health sweep after pushing, not by anyone reporting a problem** — the deploy gate did exactly its job, but only because something was actively watching for it to fail. Fixed by guarding `noindex` the same way `canonical_override` already was. Lesson: when adding a field to `posts.json` that a per-locale rendering loop reads, always ask "does this need the same locale guard as its sibling field" — don't assume a new field automatically inherits an existing neighboring field's scoping.
- **Two real gaps found in the "every workflow auto-retries after a failure" standing rule** while doing a full 39-workflow retry-coverage sweep (not because anything reported broken — proactive audit): (1) `update_football_data.yml`'s own embedded retry job called `benc-uk/workflow-dispatch@v1` without declaring `permissions: actions: write`, so every retry attempt failed with "Resource not accessible by integration" — confirmed live in that night's actual failed retry attempt. Its sibling `update_leagues.yml` has the identical job and already had the permission; straight oversight, not intentional. (2) `retry_failed.yml` (the 15-min polling safety net) was missing 7 real scheduled workflows from its `WORKFLOWS` array entirely, including `daily_seo_doctor.yml` and `seo_geo_biweekly.yml` — both explicitly named as guards in CLAUDE.md's own "SEO Health Is Continuously Self-Healing" table — meaning a silent failure on either could sit unnoticed indefinitely with zero safety net anywhere (not in this list, not in `workflow_watchdog.yml`'s watched-workflow list, no embedded retry of its own). **Lesson: "no exceptions" policies drift silently as new workflows get added over time — worth a periodic full sweep (grep every workflow filename against both retry mechanisms' coverage lists) rather than assuming coverage stays complete once set up correctly.**
- Deliberately did NOT add on-demand/manual-dispatch-only workflows (`casino_post.yml`, `match_post.yml`, `test_socials.yml`) to `retry_failed.yml` even though they also show zero coverage — that safety net retries whatever a workflow's *last run* concluded as, with no concept of "this one only runs when a human triggers it." Adding them would auto-retry a one-off manual test forever, every 15 minutes, until someone noticed and manually cancelled it.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-02, later same session) — the tweet-length fix confirmed live, end-to-end

- The character-weighting fix (`agents/python/utils/tweet_text.py`) was validated on its first live run after deploy, at 00:18 UTC — a retry fired by the safety net only ~65 min after the previous failure, not the next scheduled slot. Log confirmed the exact chain: `Post button enabled after polling: True` (was `False` on every single run for 2+ days before this fix) → `✓ Tweet posted via browser — CreateTweet confirmed (HTTP 200)`. This closes out the X posting investigation that spanned two fixes across two days: 2026-08-01's polling fix was necessary (it produced the diagnostics that ruled out the timing theory) but not sufficient; the real fix was the character-weighting bug found by reading Twitter's own published config rather than continuing to guess at UI/timing explanations.
- Also worth noting for future sessions: a live "Priority Content" agent/workflow (evergreen guide writer, closes the keyword-gap-to-content loop) was built and shipped by a different concurrent process on this same machine during this session — visible via `git log`, not something this session authored. Its first live compliance-check false positive (banned-phrase negation detection missing quoted lists) was found and fixed as part of this session's routine scanning, since it was already committed/live, not private draft work. Left its actual content/feature work untouched throughout.

### Code Patterns (2026-08-02, later same session) — the site-wide safety net had the exact bug it exists to catch, and every other workflow's retry coverage sits on top of it

- `retry_failed.yml`'s `cron: "*/15 * * * *"` was found to have real gaps of 160-230+ minutes across a 60-run sample — not a one-off. This is the same account-wide GitHub Actions scheduler unreliability already documented and fixed for `transfer_news.yml`/`live_odds.yml`/`hourly_scrape.yml`, just never applied to this workflow — and it's the one workflow whose entire job is catching *other* workflows' missed/failed schedules, so every multi-hour gap here meant a multi-hour gap in site-wide retry coverage, not just for this one workflow.
- Applied the identical fix `transfer_news.yml` already proved: wrapped the existing two check functions (`check_and_retry_failures`, `check_missed_schedules` — bodies left byte-for-byte unchanged) in a `while` loop that runs every ~14 min for up to ~5h50m per job execution, plus `concurrency: group: retry-safety-net, cancel-in-progress: true` so a fresh trigger restarts rather than piling up. Free on this repo (confirmed public via `gh repo view --json isPrivate` before committing to the higher-compute-minutes approach — would have needed a different tradeoff on a private repo).
- **Verification approach worth remembering for next time a long-running-loop workflow needs smoke-testing**: `gh run view --log` and `gh run watch` do NOT stream partial output for a step that's still in progress — logs only become available once the step/job completes, which defeats watching a 340-minute loop directly. Also deliberately did NOT re-run the actual `check_and_retry_failures()` body locally with real `gh` calls to "test" it, since that function's whole job is firing real `gh workflow run` triggers against 31 live workflows — running it standalone as a "test" would have caused genuine duplicate triggers, not a safe dry run. Instead: (1) `bash -n` syntax check, (2) the loop control-flow (timing/sleep/break logic only) isolated and tested with stubbed check functions standing in for the untouched real bodies, (3) manually dispatched the real workflow once and confirmed via `gh run view --json status,conclusion,jobs` that "Set up job" succeeded and the main step reached and stayed in `in_progress` for several minutes with no crash, then cancelled it — proof of clean startup without needing to babysit a multi-hour job or risk a spurious duplicate trigger storm.

### Code Patterns (2026-08-02) — closing the keyword-gap-to-content loop: priority queue + evergreen guide writer

- Built `agents/python/agent_content_priority.py` + `agents/python/agent_priority_writer.py` to fix a real gap: `agent5_plan.py`'s weekly editorial calendar (and the keyword research agents feeding it) never actually turned into scheduled posts — nothing consumed `weekly_plan.json` automatically. The priority agent ranks every unranked keyword gap (from `keyword_opportunities.json` + `trending_keywords.json`) by country tier (Nigeria/Kenya/Ghana/South Africa/Tanzania first, mirroring `update_countries.py`'s existing top-5 pattern) x content type into `content_priority_queue.json`; the writer actually generates posts from the top of that queue.
- **Deliberately excludes trending/news-shaped gaps and the evergreen "football betting tips today"/"best live odds" templates from auto-writing** — those are already covered by `agent_sports_blog.py`'s 3x/day news pipeline and the `/tips`/`/odds` tool pages respectively. The only genuinely uncovered ground is evergreen commercial content ("best betting sites/bonus X 2026", "how to bet online X", "safest betting apps X") — zero existing writer for that before today. Writing news-shaped content a second time would have recreated the exact 56-duplicate-title-pairs bug fixed 2026-08-01 (`utils/story_dedup.py`).
- New `agents/python/utils/site_data.py` parses `assets/shared.js`'s `COUNTRY_DATA`/`BOOKS` JS object literals (currency/regulator/payments/leagues + real per-country bookmaker names/bonuses/T&Cs) into Python dicts, read-only. No JSON equivalent of this data exists anywhere in the repo, and it's the exact data the site already renders to visitors, so grounding LLM-generated guide content in it (rather than letting the model invent bonus figures) keeps content consistent with what's actually on the page. The parser correctly handles this file's mix of single- and double-quoted strings — `shared.js` switches to double quotes specifically for values containing an apostrophe (e.g. `"Nigeria's No.1..."`) — by extracting all string literals to placeholders first (regex alternation `'...'|"..."`, each type matched by its own delimiter) before quoting unquoted keys, so the apostrophe-inside-a-string is never mistaken for a delimiter. A naive "replace all single quotes with double quotes" pass corrupts everything downstream of the first such apostrophe — confirmed by triggering exactly that bug on the first attempt before fixing it.
- `generate_priority_post()` in the writer is deliberately NOT a reuse of `agent_sports_blog.py`'s `generate_post()`/`SYSTEM_PROMPT` — that engine hard-requires real news headlines ("only write about stories explicitly listed in the provided headlines"), which an evergreen commercial keyword has none of. Built a parallel prompt grounded in `site_data.py` + `utils/serp_research.research()` instead, but reused its storage (`load_posts`/`save_posts`), image (`ensure_feature_image`), and fact-check (`check_post` — trivially passes since these posts carry no `_source_items`, matching that function's own documented "nothing to verify is not a failure" behaviour) machinery rather than duplicating it.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-02) — the priority writer's first live run surfaced three real bugs in one shot, all fixed same session

- **A brand-new content-generation prompt cannot be trusted without a deterministic post-generation quality gate, even a carefully written one.** The first real run (manually triggered to verify before letting the 2-hour cron run unsupervised — exactly the right call) produced a 453-word post against a 1000-word-minimum instruction, and used `#### Q:` (four hashes) for FAQ questions instead of the required `### ` (three) — `markdown_to_html()` in `gen_blog_post_pages.py` doesn't process H4 as a heading at all, so `#### Q: What is...` leaked as literal visible text in a `<p>` tag on the live page. The 2026-07-26 fix that strips `Q:`/`A:` labels only touches the `<h3>`/`<p>` branches, so a wrong heading level slipped straight past it too. Fixed with `_validate_body()` — a deterministic word-count/FAQ-format/final-line check — with one automatic retry (specific corrective feedback listing exactly what failed) before skipping the item entirely rather than publishing a bad draft. **Lesson: prompting alone cannot guarantee structural compliance from an LLM, especially under a weak fallback model (see next entry) — any new content-generation agent needs a code-level validation gate, not just careful prompt wording, before its first unsupervised run.**
- **A keyword "gap" (SifuFinds doesn't rank for this exact search query) does not mean SifuFinds has no page on the topic.** The very first target the priority queue picked — "best betting sites Nigeria 2026" — turned out to already have a legacy post at that exact slug, predating this pipeline entirely. Nothing checked for an existing page before queuing a fresh write; left unfixed, the very next run would have produced a near-duplicate and recreated the 2026-08-01 duplicate-content bug on a new axis (evergreen, not news). Fixed with `_already_covered()` in `agent_priority_writer.py`: scans existing post titles/slugs for the same country + core keyword phrase before writing, skips (and records the skip reason in `priority_writer_state.json`) rather than generating. Live count the first time this ran for real: 4 of 19 writer-actionable queue items (all four Tier-1 "best betting sites" keywords) were already covered — nearly a quarter of the initial backlog. **Lesson: "we don't rank for X" and "we have no content about X" are different claims — a content-gap pipeline needs to check the second before acting on the first.**
- **This workflow was subject to the exact same GitHub Actions cron-drop bug already being fixed elsewhere in the repo this same night, and nobody connected the two until checking why the 2-hour schedule only fired once in ~5 hours.** A concurrent session fixed `retry_failed.yml` for this same issue (measured 160-230+ minute gaps on a nominally "every 15 minutes" workflow) literally hours before this workflow's own first scheduled tick — `agent_priority_content.yml`'s `"0 */2 * * *"` cron only actually fired once between 00:23 and 05:34, and that one run got killed by the then-20-minute job timeout mid-generation (a Groq-quota-exhausted attempt falls through to `llm.py`'s local Ollama fallback, which pulls a fresh ~2GB model on every ephemeral runner — genuinely can exceed 20 minutes). Fixed by applying the exact same proven pattern `transfer_news.yml`/`retry_failed.yml` already validated: a self-perpetuating internal loop (own ~2h clock, up to ~5h50m per job, `timeout 1500` wrapping each write attempt so one slow Ollama pull can't block the loop) instead of depending on GitHub to deliver every tick. **Lesson: when a new scheduled workflow is added to this repo, check whether the account-wide cron-drop issue (documented in `transfer_news.yml`'s file header) applies to it too, rather than discovering it independently hours or days later — any workflow whose cadence matters (not just the historically-affected 5/15-minute ones) is a candidate.**
- Also confirmed live: `llm.py`'s local Ollama fallback (built and merged 2026-08-01, previously untested in a real Groq-quota-exhausted scenario) does work end-to-end — it installed, pulled `llama3.2:3b`, and returned text — but that text was low enough quality (654 words, no FAQ section at all on one attempt) that this agent's quality gate correctly rejected it twice in a row rather than publish. This is the tier's first real production exercise; expected per its own docstring ("a last resort that produces *something* instead of nothing, not a quality-equivalent replacement"), but worth knowing concretely how much weaker the 3B fallback is versus Groq's hosted models for any future agent considering how strict to make its own quality gate.

### Market Intelligence (2026-08-02) — first full-coverage ranking snapshot and content-gap inventory

- Consumed the previous session's 51-keyword baseline (0/51 ranking) and the priority queue built from it: 19 of those gaps are evergreen commercial keywords with zero existing writer, of which 4 (the Tier-1 countries' "best betting sites X 2026") already have pre-pipeline legacy posts that just aren't ranking yet for that exact phrasing — a distinct problem (on-page SEO / content depth on an existing page) from "no content exists," worth a human/future-session pass specifically on those 4 pages' existing copy rather than assuming new content is the only lever. The other 15 actionable gaps (best bonus / how-to-bet / safest-apps across Nigeria, Kenya, Ghana, South Africa, Tanzania, plus best-sites for the remaining 18 countries) are genuine content gaps this pipeline is now writing against automatically, prioritised Tier-1 countries first.

- **Post-fix check-in (2026-08-02, ~09:17 UTC):** confirmed the self-perpetuating loop fix works — the actual `"0 */2 * * *"` schedule fired at 08:33 (previously it only fired once in 5+ hours), and its first internal cycle completed cleanly at 08:59 (commit `0e92a4fc0`, author `priority-writer@sifufinds.com`). That cycle only touched `content_priority_queue.json` (re-ranked; grew to 71+ tracked gaps as the daily keyword-research agents kept adding more) — zero new posts, `priority_writer_state.json` untouched. Most likely cause: Groq's shared quota was still exhausted at that point (confirmed exhausted as of the 00:33 UTC test run) and Ollama-fallback drafts kept failing `_validate_body()`, same failure mode as the very first test run, correctly producing nothing rather than bad content. Cannot get more detail without waiting for the ~5h50m job to fully finish (GitHub Actions doesn't stream partial logs for an in-progress step — confirmed by another concurrent session's notes above, and re-confirmed here). Sibling daily agents (`agent_keyword_research.yml` last run 04:51 UTC, `agent_trending_keywords.yml` last run 05:21 UTC) both healthy/`success`, correctly feeding the queue. Spot-checked the 4 already-covered legacy "best betting sites" posts' on-page basics (title/meta/H1) while waiting — all structurally fine (proper title tags, meta descriptions, single H1), so their non-ranking isn't a technical SEO bug, more likely competitive/authority (Goal.com, mybettingsites.com etc. dominate) or, for Ghana (730 words) and Tanzania (739 words) specifically, thin content that `agent_content_backfill.py`'s daily auto-heal hasn't reached yet (confirmed: neither slug present in `content_backfill_state.json`'s processed set) — left untouched rather than hand-patching, since that's an existing scheduled system's job, not a gap needing new code. Did NOT force additional manual `workflow_dispatch` runs to get more log detail, since `cancel-in-progress: true` means a new dispatch would cancel the currently-sleeping loop and reset its 2-hour cycle timer for no real benefit — better to let subsequent internal cycles run and check again once Groq's quota has had more time to recover.

- **Second check-in (2026-08-02, ~09:59 UTC):** still zero priority-writer posts published — but this is expected, not a stall: cycle 1 (08:33-08:59) took ~26 min, so with the 2-hour internal clock its next cycle isn't due until ~10:33 UTC; only ~33 min had passed since the last check-in against a ~94-minute sleep window. `fact_check_flags.json`'s recent entries are all from the unrelated transfer-news pipeline (agent_transfer_post.py/agent_sports_blog.py flagging speculative odds claims in transfer articles) — nothing there implicates the priority writer, consistent with it carrying no `_source_items` and never reaching that fact-checker's flagging path. General health sweep: `agent_keyword_research.yml`/`agent_trending_keywords.yml` still healthy. Found `breaking_news.yml` had 2 recent `cancelled` runs (09:10 schedule, 07:28 manual dispatch) — left uninvestigated since `retry_failed.yml`'s newly-fixed self-perpetuating loop is confirmed actively running (started 09:11, still `in_progress` ~48 min in) and `workflow_watchdog.yml` is firing every few minutes and correctly finding nothing to act on (`skipped` conclusions) — this class of issue already has dedicated coverage per the repo's standing auto-retry rule, not something this session's task needs to duplicate. `pages-build-deployment` (GitHub's own auto-workflow, unrelated to this site's actual Hostinger deploy pipeline) also showed a few cancelled runs — benign, expected when multiple pushes land close together, not investigated further as out of scope.

- **Third check-in (2026-08-02, ~11:06 UTC): 2 consecutive real cycles (08:59, 10:59) both produced zero posts** — content_priority_queue.json re-ranked correctly both times (still healthy input), but agent_priority_writer.py wrote nothing either cycle. Investigating why hit a real, separate bug: **this workflow never committed `agents/python/agent_log.json`, so every GitHub Actions run's log entries were lost when the ephemeral runner tore down** — this session's local `agent_log.json` only had entries from its own earlier manual local test runs, nothing from any of the actual scheduled cycles, making cross-run diagnosis impossible after the fact. Worse, a dirty tracked file (agent_log.json gets written every cycle regardless of outcome) makes the next iteration's `git pull --rebase` fail outright, forcing the `reset --hard` fallback on every single cycle instead of only on a genuine push conflict — real if modest wasted overhead on a loop already tight against its 2-hour cadence. Fixed: `agent_priority_content.yml` now stages `agent_log.json` when there's real content to commit, and explicitly `git checkout --`s it back clean when there isn't (commit `ce8b6624b`). Since the loop only re-reads the workflow file at job start (not mid-loop), triggered a fresh `workflow_dispatch` (cancels the currently-sleeping scheduled job via `cancel-in-progress: true`, no real work lost since it was just sleeping) to get this fix live sooner and produce a diagnosable cycle rather than waiting ~90 more minutes for the natural next tick. Per the explicit 3-cycles threshold set for this investigation: 2 consecutive zero-output cycles so far is suggestive but not yet conclusive — deliberately held off changing `agent_priority_writer.py`'s quality bar (e.g. a lower word-count floor specifically under Ollama) without clearer evidence of *why* cycles are empty, since loosening the gate speculatively risks reintroducing the exact thin-content bug fixed earlier tonight. Next check should have real `agent_log.json` entries to read instead of inferring from commit diffs alone.
- Also noted: another concurrent Claude Code session is live-editing `.github/workflows/breaking_news.yml` right now (uncommitted in the shared working tree) to fix the *exact same class of bug* — Ollama-fallback wall-clock time exceeding an old job timeout, confirmed by their in-progress comment referencing `AGENT-KNOWLEDGE.md`'s 2026-08-01 Ollama entry directly. Left it untouched and preserved it correctly through this session's own `git stash -u` / merge / `stash pop` cycles (verified present after each). Cross-validates this session's own diagnosis of the same failure mode in `agent_priority_content.yml` independently.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-02, fourth check-in) — two more real bugs in the priority-content loop, both found by refusing to accept "wait and see" past 3 empty cycles

- **My own previous "fix" for missing GitHub Actions diagnostics was a silent no-op.** Assumed omitting `agents/python/agent_log.json` from the workflow's `git add` was an oversight and force-added it — except it's deliberately listed in `agents/python/.gitignore` (the real durable log store is a Supabase mirror `utils/logger.py` writes to, which this workflow has no access to query). `git add` on a gitignored path silently no-ops without `-f`, and the surrounding `2>/dev/null || true` masked even a warning. Result: three consecutive real cycles (08:59, 10:59, 11:47) all committed *only* `content_priority_queue.json`, and this session still had zero visibility into why `agent_priority_writer.py` wrote nothing each time — the exact problem the earlier "fix" was supposed to solve, still unsolved, discovered only by directly checking `git check-ignore -v` on the path after the second post-"fix" cycle still showed no log entries. **Real fix**: `generate_priority_post()` now returns `(post, reason)` instead of just `post`, with a specific machine-readable reason at every failure exit (`quality_gate: <exact validation failures>`, `fact_check: <flags>`, `missing_output_markers`, `no_site_data`, `error: <exception>`). `run()` persists this every cycle as `priority_writer_state.json`'s new `last_run` key (`candidates_available`/`attempted`/`written` counts + a per-item attempt log) — that file is NOT gitignored and already gets committed normally, so this finally gives real cross-run diagnostics without depending on Supabase access or waiting hours for a full job log. **Lesson: verify a "fix" for missing observability by actually checking the file showed up in the next real commit, not by trusting the code looks right — `2>/dev/null || true` on a git command is exactly the kind of pattern that quietly converts "this doesn't work" into "this doesn't even tell you it's not working."**
- **`cancel-in-progress: true`, copied from `transfer_news.yml`'s proven pattern, was actively counterproductive here because the two workflows' internal cycle times are wildly different.** `transfer_news.yml` tolerates frequent cancel-and-restart because each internal cycle is ~5 minutes; this workflow's single write attempt can take up to 25 minutes (the `timeout 1500` cap). Confirmed live: a manually-dispatched run started 11:06 UTC was killed by the *next* cron tick at 11:21 UTC — only 15 minutes later, nowhere near the nominal 2-hour spacing, confirming (again) that this account's GitHub Actions scheduler delivers ticks erratically rather than on clean boundaries. Under `cancel-in-progress: true` a tick landing inside an active 25-minute write window kills it before it can finish even one cycle — a livelock risk that gets worse, not better, the more reliable GitHub's delivery becomes (more frequent ticks = more chances to interrupt). Fixed: switched to `cancel-in-progress: false` — a new tick now queues instead of killing in-flight work, and GitHub's own concurrency semantics already collapse a backlog of queued ticks down to just the latest, so this doesn't reintroduce the pileup risk `transfer_news.yml`'s own comments warn about for its choice of `true`. **Lesson: a proven pattern from one workflow isn't automatically safe to copy into another — check whether the specific parameter that made it safe there (here: cycle duration vs. expected trigger frequency) actually holds for the new use case.**
- Both fixes pushed at commit `1f4f7f12f`. Since the workflow's self-perpetuating loop does `git pull --rebase origin main` at the start of every internal iteration (refreshing the actual Python scripts on the runner, not just data files), the *currently running* job (started 11:21, not restarted) will pick up both fixes automatically on its own next cycle rather than needing a fresh dispatch — confirmed by re-reading the loop step's own bash rather than assumed. Given `cancel-in-progress` is now `false`, a fresh manual dispatch right now would only queue behind the current ~5h50m job rather than replace it, so deliberately did not trigger one; next check-in should target after that job's next natural cycle (~13:21 UTC: this job started 11:21, its first cycle finished 11:47 (~26min), so its ~94min sleep window ends ~13:21) rather than sooner.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-02, fifth check-in) — the actual root cause of every empty cycle since this agent existed, found by refusing to trust "probably just quota exhaustion" a fourth time

- **`run()`'s batch loop had no try/except around `generate_priority_post()`, so an `AIProvidersExhausted` exception (deliberately re-raised inside that function, copied verbatim from `agent_sports_blog.py`'s pattern) crashed the whole script silently, before the state-write code from the previous check-in's fix could ever execute.** This is almost certainly why every real cycle since this agent was built (00:14, 00:33, 08:59, 10:59, 11:47, 13:47 — six cycles across the whole night) committed only `content_priority_queue.json` with zero trace of what happened inside `agent_priority_writer.py`: the crash happened before either the old (broken) `agent_log.json` approach or the new (working, but never-reached) `priority_writer_state.json.last_run` write. The crash was invisible to the workflow layer too — the bash step wraps the call as `timeout 1500 python agent_priority_writer.py ...` with no exit-code check under `set +e`, so the GitHub Actions job's own conclusion was unaffected and neither `retry_failed.yml` nor `workflow_watchdog.yml` had any failed run to notice. **Verified by direct reproduction, not inference**: stubbed `generate_priority_post` to raise `AIProvidersExhausted` directly and confirmed `run()` crashed with exactly zero state written — then applied the fix (try/except around the call, log the reason as `"AIProvidersExhausted"`, `break` the batch early since every remaining item would fail identically) and re-ran the same stub, confirming `last_run` now persists correctly even in this exact failure mode.
- **Why `agent_sports_blog.py`'s "let it propagate" design doesn't transplant cleanly**: that agent's own docstring is explicit about the reasoning — an uncaught `AIProvidersExhausted` exits the script non-zero, which lets GitHub Actions mark the job as failed, which is what `retry_failed.yml`/`workflow_watchdog.yml` watch for. But `agent_priority_content.yml`'s loop never propagates a sub-step's exit code to the job level at all (by design, so a bad cycle doesn't kill the whole multi-hour loop) — so the exact behavior that makes propagation useful for `agent_sports_blog.py` (a failed job that gets noticed and retried) doesn't exist in this workflow's shape. **Lesson: copying a defensive pattern ("let this specific exception propagate") from one agent to another is only safe if the calling context that pattern depends on is also the same — here, "propagate up to a script exit that something is watching" quietly became "propagate up to a loop iteration nothing is watching," turning a deliberate fail-loud design into an accidental fail-silent one.**
- **Bigger-picture lesson from this whole multi-check-in saga (2nd through 5th check-ins)**: three consecutive "plausible" explanations (Groq quota exhaustion, then a mis-identified gitignore issue, then a genuine but secondary concurrency bug) were each individually reasonable and each partially true, but none of them was *the* actual reason zero posts were being written — that required directly reproducing the crash with a stub rather than continuing to reason from commit-diff absence alone. When a system produces "nothing" repeatedly and every individual hypothesis checks out but the symptom doesn't change, escalate to direct reproduction (mocking the suspected failure path and running the real code against it) rather than accumulating more plausible-sounding theories.
- Pushed at `61f5ce802`. Same self-updating mechanism applies: the currently-running job (started 11:21 UTC, still going) will pick this up on its own next `git pull --rebase` at the start of its next internal cycle, no restart needed.

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-02, overnight monitoring) — the Ollama fallback fix from earlier this same session had its own unintended side effect: job-level timeouts sized before it existed

- While doing routine overnight health checkpoints (every ~45 min after the main round of fixes), noticed `breaking_news.yml` had two runs that day conclude as `"cancelled"` rather than `"failure"` — easy to miss since a cancelled run doesn't read as broken the way a failure does. Dug into the actual step timeline (`gh run view --json jobs`) rather than just the conclusion: both runs completed their FIRST category's `generate_post()` successfully, then got killed by the job's own `timeout-minutes: 15` partway through the SECOND category — with `Commit and push new articles` never reached (skipped), meaning a fully-written article was silently discarded both times.
- **Root cause traces straight back to this same session's earlier fix**: the local Ollama fallback tier added to `llm.py` (2026-08-01) sits between Groq and Claude/Gemini and adds real wall-clock time the *first* time any given job invocation falls all the way through Groq — a one-time install + ~2GB model pull, then genuinely slow CPU-only inference. `breaking_news.yml`'s `timeout-minutes: 15` was sized (per its own code comment) against Groq's RPM-retry cost alone, before Ollama existed as a tier — the new fallback tier's benefit (produces content instead of nothing) came with a real timeout-budget cost nobody had accounted for yet.
- **Checked every other workflow whose agent imports `llm.py`** rather than just fixing the one that was reported/noticed, matching this session's established pattern of chasing a bug to every sibling call site: `content_backfill.yml` and `agent_translate.yml` both showed their *first-ever* cancellation the very next time their once-daily schedule fired after the Ollama fix landed — same signature, same fix (15min→40min). `seo_backlinks.yml` (hourly, `timeout-minutes: 10`) was already at 3-of-5-recent-runs cancelled and getting worse. `brand_scraper.yml`/`brand_discovery.yml` also import `llm.py` but showed zero cancellations (their existing 20min budget already had enough headroom) — left untouched, since "imports llm.py" alone isn't sufficient justification to change a timeout that isn't actually causing observed harm.
- **Lesson for any future change to a shared fallback/retry chain that adds latency (not just this Ollama tier — any future tier added to `llm.py`, or any shared dependency multiple workflows call into)**: changing the *worst-case latency* of a shared library function is a change to every caller's effective timeout budget, not just a self-contained code change. After landing something like this, check `timeout-minutes` across every workflow that transitively depends on it, the same way you'd check for a duplicated bug pattern — a job silently killed by its own timeout conclude as `"cancelled"`, not `"failure"`, so it will not show up in most `conclusion == failure` health checks (including this repo's own `retry_failed.yml`, which only re-triggers on `"failure"` — a `"cancelled"` conclusion is currently invisible to that safety net too, a further gap worth remembering if it recurs).

### Errors to Never Repeat (2026-08-02, later same session) — a filter-condition typo turned a 17-post fix into an 813-post regeneration; recovery process worth remembering

- **The mistake**: while fixing a real bug (a title containing U+2011 NON-BREAKING HYPHEN rendered a tofu box in generated feature-image PNGs — found via visual spot-check, not any automated check, since the font gap only shows up as a rendered artifact), the Python one-liner meant to find the ~17 affected posts used a character-membership check that was supposed to test for U+202F NARROW NO-BREAK SPACE but instead matched a **plain ASCII space** (a typo introduced when re-typing the check for the actual regeneration pass, after already verifying the correct version moments earlier). Since every post title contains spaces, this matched all 813 posts and kicked off a full site-wide feature-image regeneration — each with a live photo-search call — a massively larger and riskier operation than intended, silently, with no error or warning (the code was syntactically and logically valid, just checking the wrong condition).
- **Caught by**: noticing the progress output counter climbing past where 17 items should have finished ("[32/138]" style continuing well beyond the low double digits expected), not by any assertion or safety check — there wasn't one. Killed the process immediately with `pkill -f`.
- **Recovery approach, worth reusing verbatim next time a runaway batch operation needs to be untangled from legitimate concurrent changes**: rather than trying to reconstruct "what should have happened," (1) derived the exact correct target list fresh from source data (re-ran the corrected filter against `posts.json`), (2) extracted the exact list of what a DIFFERENT, legitimate, already-completed batch operation earlier in the same session had touched (parsed its own log output for a definitive slug list, since that run's log lines were still in a file), (3) computed `git status` for every changed file in the affected directory, (4) for every changed file, mapped it back to its owning post's slug via `posts.json`'s own `feature_image` field (not by filename pattern-matching, which would have been fragile) and checked that slug against the union of the two legitimate sets, (5) restored (`git checkout --` for tracked, `rm` for untracked) anything that didn't map to a legitimate slug. This produced an exact, verifiable partition (kept / restored / unmapped) rather than a best-effort guess.
- **A second-order casualty found only by checking, not assumed**: the abrupt `pkill` mid-write left 3 files in a bad state — 1 legitimately-referenced image outright deleted (mid-write when the process was killed) and, separately, 2 *pre-existing* missing images unrelated to any of tonight's work (confirmed absent from `HEAD` itself, i.e. broken before this session touched anything — only surfaced because the recovery process's verification step checked "does every post's `feature_image` path actually exist on disk" as a general sweep, not just "did I revert cleanly"). All three regenerated properly rather than left broken. **Lesson: after killing an interrupted write, don't just verify the intended damage is undone — verify nothing else is now in a half-written state, and while doing that sweep, don't assume everything else was already fine either.**
- **A live root-cause reminder about the audit tooling itself**: `audit_feature_images.py --regenerate` completing successfully does not mean the audit will show 0 suspects afterward — its detection heuristic flags based on "does this post have a blocklisted tag anywhere in its tags AND is the current image in photo-mode," which can't distinguish "this photo came from the blocklisted tag" from "this photo came from a different, legitimate tag that happens to coexist with a blocklisted one." A post with an `NBA` tag and a real, correct photo will always re-flag as a "suspect" even after a perfectly correct regeneration. The only way to actually confirm success is visual spot-check of the regenerated PNGs, not re-running the audit and expecting a clean report.
