**Also confirmed (not a bug, a process note)**: this repo's local automated deploy pipeline committed and pushed both new files under generic `deploy: <timestamp> UTC` messages mid-session, before they were finished being tested/fixed — twice, once mid-fix. `git diff HEAD` came back empty for both files by the end of the session (the second auto-commit happened to catch the fully-fixed state), so nothing was lost, but this is a live reminder that the "auto-deploy is not a substitute for committing your own work" caveat in the git-workflow standing rule is real: don't assume a clean `git status` mid-session means nothing has changed since you last looked — check `git log` for unexpected commits before concluding your own commit is still pending.

**Not built (deliberately out of scope per the user's own correction)**: no website page, no public dashboard. `--stats` prints an accuracy report to the terminal only. If a public-facing accuracy dashboard is wanted later, `aggregate_stats()` already returns exactly the numbers needed — it would just need a small render layer, not a data-model change.

### 2026-08-21 (same-day, fourth pass) — After the llm.py crash fix, drafts generate again but the fact-checker still rejects most of them: a real, separate content-quality gap, not a new bug

User reported "still no new blogs" a few hours after the llm.py crash fix above was confirmed live. Manually triggered `breaking_news.yml` again and pulled the full log rather than assume the earlier fix was somehow undone: confirmed the crash itself is genuinely gone (every category now correctly logs a full generation attempt — a real photo gets assigned, etc. — instead of an instant `model_not_found` exception), but 3/3 categories still produced 0 published posts that cycle, and `blog/posts.json` had not gained a single new entry since the one post at 10:23 UTC.

**Diagnosis**: the drafts *are* being generated (confirmed: no more crash), but `agent_fact_checker.py`'s `check_post()` gate — unchanged, last touched 2026-08-16, working exactly as designed — is correctly flagging nearly every one for fabricated specifics: invented odds ranges ("BTTS markets around 1.70–1.80"), invented match predictions ("Historically, Tests between these two sides finish 27‑24 or 31‑28"), invented injury/quote details. Traced one flagged draft back to its source: no "Groq model exhausted, trying next" log line appeared before the fact-check result, meaning the *very first* configured Groq model (`openai/gpt-oss-120b`, the best of the 3 models this session swapped in to replace the retired ones) produced the hallucinated content directly — this is not a fallback-tier problem, it's the current best available free Groq model itself being noticeably worse at following `agent_sports_blog.py`'s explicit, repeated "NEVER invent an odds number / a transfer fee / a statistic" instructions than the model it replaced.

**This is not a bug to fix by loosening the fact-checker.** The strict "flag any specific odds number" rule in `FACT_CHECK_SYSTEM_PROMPT` exists precisely because of a past incident (documented elsewhere in this file, 2026-08-15) where hundreds of fabricated odds tables reached publication under an earlier, looser policy. The gate rejecting most drafts from the current model lineup is the gate doing its job — the honest tradeoff is lower publish volume until draft quality improves, not weaker fact-checking to force volume back up.

**Partial mitigation applied**: `prefer_accuracy=True` (documented in `llm.py` as "swap g4f/Ollama tier order so the more careful, locally-run Ollama model is tried before the weaker hosted g4f aggregator") was previously hardcoded to only the `transfers` category in `agent_sports_blog.py`'s `generate_post()`, on the theory that transfer fees were uniquely fact-fragile. That's no longer true — the current model swap makes every category equally exposed. Widened to `prefer_accuracy=True` unconditionally in `agent_sports_blog.py`, and added the same to `agent_country_trending_writer.py` and `agent_priority_writer.py` (both had their own separate `ask()`/`ask_long()` calls defaulting to `False`; `agent_fabricated_content_fixer.py` already had it unconditionally). **Explicitly a partial fix, not a full one**: `prefer_accuracy` only changes the ordering *after* all 4 Groq models are exhausted — in the traced example, Groq's first model succeeded (produced non-empty text) on the first try, so this change would not have prevented that specific hallucination. It only helps the subset of cycles where Groq's models come back empty/erroring and the call falls through to g4f/Ollama, where Ollama's stronger instruction-following gives a better shot at a clean draft.

**What would actually close the remaining gap** (not done this pass — needs live verification this sandbox can't perform, see the `403 Access denied` note in the crash-fix entry above): Groq's live model list (confirmed via the same `GET /v1/models` call) also includes `groq/compound` and `groq/compound-mini` — Groq's own tool-grounded "agentic" models, which can in principle look up real data instead of inventing it, directly addressing the actual failure mode observed here (fabricated specifics) rather than just reordering fallback tiers. Not added speculatively: compound models can behave differently in response shape/latency and this needs a real generation test against actual output before trusting it in the pipeline, which requires access this environment didn't have working. Worth trying next if post volume is still low after this pass.

**Errors to Never Repeat / lessons for next time this class of report comes in**: (1) "no new blog posts" can have two completely independent causes stacked on top of each other — a hard crash (fixed first) and a soft quality-gate rejection rate (found only by reading full logs *after* confirming the crash was gone, not by assuming one fix explains the whole symptom). Don't declare "no new posts" resolved just because the specific exception that was crashing the pipeline is gone — verify an actual new post lands, not just that the process runs cleanly. (2) When a fact-check/quality gate starts rejecting more content than before, check whether the *content quality* changed (here: a different underlying LLM) before assuming the *gate* regressed — tightening/loosening a deliberately strict safety gate that exists because of a documented past incident should not be the default response to a drop in throughput.

### 2026-08-21 (fifth pass) — Added fun/engaging copy + real club crest images to the prediction agent; found the iCloud-duplicate-file bug class hitting `.git/` internals, not just content files

User asked for the prediction agent's social copy to match a pasted example (playful, self-aware tone, e.g. joking when the model calls the same scoreline repeatedly) plus real club logos, and for "all of" a gameweek's fixtures to be covered.

**Copy**: rewrote `build_telegram_post`/`build_facebook_post`/`build_instagram_post`/`build_twitter_post`/`build_threads_post`/`build_tiktok_script` in `agent_predictions.py` around a new `_dominant_score()` helper — deliberately a *majority* detector (>=60% of a round sharing one scoreline, not only a literal 100% match), since a real live batch came back 9 draws out of 10, not all 10, and the fun framing needed to land on that real case too, not just the hypothetical all-identical one.

**Real club crests**: new `agents/python/utils/gameweek_card.py` composites a branded PNG with every fixture's real crest next to the predicted score, using ESPN's own public CDN (`team.logo` in the same scoreboard endpoint already used for grading — confirmed live, e.g. `https://a.espncdn.com/i/teamlogos/soccer/500/360.png` for Man United) rather than any invented/guessed image URL. Two bugs caught by actually looking at the rendered image, not just running the code: (1) Tottenham's navy crest was nearly invisible against the card's dark green background — fixed with a light circular backdrop composited behind every crest regardless of its own colour; (2) an emoji in a PIL-drawn footer rendered as a tofu box (the system TrueType font has no colour-emoji glyphs) — removed rather than relying on emoji in any PIL `draw.text()` call.

**A real, non-cosmetic bug found while wiring up crests**: `Brentford vs Spurs` failed to match ESPN's event at all (`_team_match_score("Spurs", "Tottenham Hotspur")` = 0, zero token overlap). This isn't just a missing-logo issue — the identical matching function is what `prediction_store.fetch_final_score()` uses for post-match grading, so this fixture would have silently never been graded either. Fixed with a small shared nickname-expansion table (`_NICKNAME_EXPANSIONS` in `prediction_store.py`: Spurs, Wolves, Hammers, Toffees, etc. — deliberately excludes ambiguous ones like "Reds"/"Blues" shared by multiple clubs) applied inside `_tokens()` in both `prediction_store.py` and `agent_predictions.py`'s own copy of the same helper. **Lesson: when two independent copies of a fuzzy-matching helper exist in a codebase (a known, accepted pattern in this repo — see the 2026-08-21 fourth-pass entry above), a correctness fix to one's matching logic needs to be checked against the other too, since both feed real, consequential behaviour (grading + image lookup) off the same kind of real-world name variation.**

**Separately, an infrastructure bug**: `git fetch` started failing outright with `fatal: bad object refs/remotes/origin/main 2` mid-session. Root cause: a stray `.git/refs/remotes/origin/main 2` file (literal space + "2" in the name) sitting alongside the real `.git/refs/remotes/origin/main` — the exact same class of bug as the already-documented 2026-08-14 "39 stray sync-conflict duplicate `index 2.html` files" incident (this repo lives under `~/Desktop`, which iCloud Drive syncs, and concurrent processes writing the same file trigger macOS's "file already exists" duplicate-naming behaviour), but this is the first time it's been seen hitting a *git-internal* ref file rather than repository content. Confirmed safe to delete before removing it: both files just contain a single commit SHA, and the stray one (`4fd1690ae...`) was simply a stale snapshot of an earlier `origin/main` position, fully superseded and reconstructable via a clean `git fetch` — same "verify zero unique content before removing" standard as the original incident. **Worth remembering**: this bug class isn't confined to visible repo files; if `git fetch`/`git pull` ever fails with a "bad object" or "broken ref name" error on this machine, check `find .git/refs -name "* [0-9]"` for a duplicate-named ref before assuming a deeper repo corruption.

**Also reconfirmed** (same pattern as the fourth-pass entry): this repo's many scheduled automations (leagues/odds/doctor/backlink/growth/transfer-news/twitter/content-priority pipelines) kept committing and pushing to `main` independently throughout this session — every `git push` in this session needed a `git fetch` + `git merge origin/main` first, all of which merged cleanly with zero conflicts since the automated commits never touch the same files this task was editing. Don't be alarmed by a rejected push here; it's expected background activity, not a sign of a problem, and force-pushing would be the wrong response.

### 2026-08-21 (sixth pass) — Added a crowd-vs-model prediction poll game; found Telegram channels flatly reject non-anonymous polls

User asked for two things on top of the fun-copy/crest-image work above: a glossier "iGaming"-style visual for the gameweek card, and a genuine interactive prediction game for followers (asked for "both" via a clarifying question that offered visual-polish vs. interactive-game as the two readings).

**Visual**: `utils/gameweek_card.py` reworked from flat alternating rows to an odds-slip look — full-card vertical gradient, a diagonal gold accent band in the header (same device `utils/social_image.py` already uses), the predicted score in a gold-bordered rounded pill, and confidence colour-coded (green >=70%, gold 60-69%, grey below) instead of plain white text.

**A real platform constraint discovered while building the interactive game, not something guessable from docs alone**: the original design was a non-anonymous Telegram poll (Home/Draw/Away) per round, with `getUpdates`/`poll_answer` used to record which individual follower voted what, feeding a per-follower points leaderboard. `sendPoll` rejected this outright at the API level the moment it was tried live: `"Bad Request: non-anonymous polls can't be sent to channel chats"` — Telegram channels (as opposed to groups) only ever support anonymous polls, and an anonymous poll's `poll_answer` updates carry no voter identity at all. This makes a genuine per-follower leaderboard structurally impossible on a channel, not just hard — no amount of additional code fixes it.

**Redesigned around what channels actually support**: `utils/prediction_game.py` now sends an anonymous poll on the round's highest-confidence pick (`pick_best()`), and at grading time calls `stopPoll` (which *does* return final aggregate vote counts even for anonymous polls — this is the one piece of real per-poll data a channel exposes) to compare the crowd's top pick against both the real result and SifuFinds' own call, then posts a "crowd vs model" result message and keeps a running season tally (`--game-tally`). This is a smaller but entirely real feature — no invented per-user data, no dead code path that silently does nothing.

**Also caught by testing, not by reading the diff**: the first version of `prediction_game.py` computed `REPO_ROOT` with three `.parent` calls instead of the four every sibling module in `agents/python/utils/` uses (it's four levels from `agents/python/utils/<file>.py` to the actual repo root) — would have silently written `data/prediction_game.json` to a nonexistent `agents/data/` path forever, since `Path.write_text` doesn't error loudly for a bad *relative* structure the way it does for a genuinely missing directory... actually it did throw `FileNotFoundError` immediately, which is what caught it — but the lesson holds: always copy the exact `.parent` chain from a working sibling file in the same directory depth rather than re-deriving it by eye, since an off-by-one here fails silently in spirit even when Python happens to raise on it.

**Lesson for future platform-integration work in this repo**: verify a third-party platform's stated capability against a real live call before designing a feature's data model around it, especially for anything involving "which user did what" — a channel vs. a group vs. a bot DM are three different permission surfaces on Telegram alone, and assuming parity between them (as the first design here did) can invalidate an entire feature's core mechanism, not just one function.

### 2026-08-22 — "/tips/ and /odds/ show wrong data" + "replace the World Cup group table on /leagues/": three unrelated real bugs, not one

User reported wrong data on `/tips/` and `/odds/`, asked for the `/leagues/` standings tab (still showing "FIFA World Cup 2026 — Group Stage") to become major-league tables instead, and asked for the anonymous per-match "Bet →" CTA on `/leagues/` (hardcoded to 1xBet) to use 22Bet/TicTacBets instead.

**Root cause #1 — WC2026 group table was structurally dead, not just stale**: today's in-fiction date (2026-08-22) is over a month past the World Cup 2026 final (July 2026), so `update_leagues.py`'s `_fd_fetch_standings()`/`_espn_fetch_wc_standings()` path was fetching a *resolved* tournament every 30 minutes forever — never wrong exactly, just permanently pointless. Replaced with `_fetch_major_league_standings()` (Premier League/La Liga/Bundesliga/Serie A/Ligue 1 via ESPN's free `.../soccer/{slug}/standings`, the same no-auth endpoint pattern the file already used for WC groups), writing `data/league_standings.json` (`{updated, leagues:[{league, table}]}`) instead of `data/wc_standings.json`. Deleted the now-orphaned `data/wc_standings.json` after confirming (`grep -rn`) nothing else in the repo referenced it. **A workflow file needed the matching fix too**: `update_leagues.yml`'s commit step did `git add data/matches_live.json data/wc_standings.json` — a literal filename, not derived from the Python script, so renaming the output file in code alone would have left the new `league_standings.json` silently never committed by CI even though local runs produced it correctly. Same class of gap as every other "fixed in one place, not the place that actually ships it" bug this file has documented before — check the workflow's own `git add` line whenever a script's output filename changes.

**Root cause #2 — `agent_live_odds.py`'s ESPN calls have been 403ing on literally every request for a long time, not just for football**: this script sets `HEADERS = {"User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 ...; live-odds-agent)"}` and passes it to every `requests.get()` against `site.api.espn.com`. `update_leagues.py` already discovered and documented (2026-08-09) that ESPN's edge 403s *any* explicitly-set User-Agent, custom or realistic-browser alike, and only the bare default `requests`/`curl` UA gets HTTP 200 — but that fix was never propagated to this sibling file. Verified live: NBA/tennis/cricket/rugby endpoints were *also* silently returning 0 events this whole time, not just the football this session was adding — this script's football omission ("EPL/La Liga/UCL are in off-season; omitted to avoid stale data", a comment that was true when written during the WC2026 window but stale itself once the leagues resumed) just happened to be the symptom that got noticed. Fixed by dropping the custom header entirely, matching `update_leagues.py`'s proven approach. **A second, independent crash surfaced immediately after fixing the 403**: ESPN's soccer scoreboard can return `"odds": [null]` (a one-element list containing `None`, not an empty list or omitted key) when a match has no odds panel yet — `map_event()`'s `odds_list[0].get(...)` then threw `AttributeError` on that one match, and because the whole per-endpoint fetch loop lived inside one try/except, that single bad match silently zeroed out *every* match for that entire league, not just itself. Fixed by filtering falsy entries out of the odds list before indexing (`[o for o in (comp.get("odds") or []) if o]`). Added the same 5 major-league ESPN endpoints (`eng.1`/`esp.1`/`ger.1`/`ita.1`/`fra.1`) this script had omitted, using keys `epl`/`laliga`/`bundesliga`/`seriea`/`ligue1` to match the (now-fixed, see below) frontend convention.

**Root cause #3 — `odds/index.html`'s own league filters were mislabelled, independent of the backend bugs above**: its client-side `ESPN_ENDPOINTS` list and the sidebar's `fsbFilt(...)` onclick handlers both had Bundesliga and Serie A hardcoded to `key:'epl'` — clicking either filter silently showed Premier League matches instead of the league you asked for. Ligue 1 had a sidebar filter button but no matching fetch endpoint at all, so it always showed nothing. Separately, the World Cup 2026 ESPN slug was typo'd as `fifa.worldcup` (should be `fifa.world`, confirmed against `update_leagues.py`'s working slug) — harmless right now only because there's no WC2026 data left to fetch either way, but a real bug in the code regardless. Fixed all four (new `bundesliga`/`seriea`/`ligue1` keys threaded through `ESPN_ENDPOINTS`, `LG_TABS`, `LG_LABELS`, and the sidebar buttons; `fifa.world` slug corrected).

**Root cause #4 — a genuine data-fabrication bug in the tips scraper, found only by reading actual scraped output, not by reading the parser code**: `agent_scrape_tips.py`'s `parse_freesupertips()` uses a line-proximity heuristic (nearest prediction-shaped line before the nearest match-shaped line) to pair a tip's predicted winner with its match — a live run produced `Borussia Dortmund vs Bayern Munich → Celta Vigo Win`, an entirely unrelated team fabricated as the "prediction" for a real match, because the page's actual card layout didn't match the parser's positional assumption for that block. Fixed with a new `_predicted_team_in_match()` guard: before accepting a "TeamX to Win" prediction, verify TeamX is actually one of the two named sides (loose containment + last-word match, to tolerate short-name variants); if it isn't, drop that tip entirely rather than publish a fabricated result. Verified against the full refreshed `data/tips.json` (251 tips) with a standalone cross-check script — 0 team/match mismatches remained afterward. **Lesson: "wrong data" complaints on a scraped/parsed page are worth testing against a live scrape before assuming the fix is just "run the refresh job" — a parser bug that silently mismatches two adjacent but unrelated fields will keep reproducing on every future scrape until the parser itself is fixed, no matter how often the data is refreshed.**

**Also fixed, smaller**: `leagues/index.html`'s per-match "Bet →" CTA was a single hardcoded `PRIMARY_BET_URL` pointing at 1xBet's affiliate link for every row on the page; changed to `PRIMARY_BET_URLS` (22Bet + TicTacBets) alternating by row index. Refreshed `data/tips.json`/`data/predictions.json`/`data/live.json`/`data/matches_live.json` via the real pipelines (`agent_scrape_tips.py`, `update_predictions.py`, `agent_live_odds.py`, `update_leagues.py`) rather than hand-editing JSON — all four already route through free-first scraping with Firecrawl as a scoped last resort per the tips/odds/leagues Firecrawl policy, so no new paid/keyed dependency was added. **Process note**: these scripts read `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY`/`ODDS_API_KEY`/etc. from `agents/python/.env` via `os.getenv()` at import time with no `load_dotenv()` call of their own (they rely on `config.py`'s `load_dotenv()` having already run in-process) — running one directly with `python3 script.py` from a fresh shell needs the env vars exported first (`export $(grep -E "^(FIRECRAWL_API_KEY|...)=" agents/python/.env)`) or Firecrawl silently never fires and you get a much thinner free-only result without any error. This is the same two-`.env`-files gotcha already documented in this file's 2026-08-14 Sanity entry, just hitting a different script.

**Errors to Never Repeat**: (1) A code comment that was accurate when written ("EPL/La Liga/UCL are in off-season; omitted to avoid stale data") can silently become false as in-story time moves forward — don't trust a comment's premise without checking today's actual date/season against it, especially in a fictional-current-date site where "now" keeps advancing past events the code was written around (WC2026 ending is the second time this exact class of bug has hit this repo — first the group table, then the odds fetcher's own stale justification for skipping football entirely). (2) When a header-related fix is documented as fixed "in this repo" (like the 2026-08-09 ESPN User-Agent fix), grep for the same header pattern across every file hitting that same host before considering the class of bug closed — `grep -rln "site.api.espn.com" --include="*.py" . | xargs grep -n "User-Agent"` would have caught this file needing the same fix immediately, rather than it surfacing only when its consequence (empty NBA/tennis/cricket/rugby/football data) got investigated for an unrelated reason.

*Last updated: 2026-08-22 by Claude Code*
